# Overview

> `compat/tmux-tracker.py write-report` generates this file. Edit the registry instead.

`compat/tmux-gaps.json` owns the backlog. The compatibility gate checks IDs, decisions,
dependencies, evidence paths, known scenarios, and the source-backed inventories described
below.

Pinned tmux commit: `d77c9dc6aa021e4bc61f0da128c591af695e6466`.

Tracked gap groups: **62**. Classified items: **479**.

- Status: open: 16, blocked: 5, accepted: 41.
- Decision: adopt: 19, native: 32, park: 2, never: 9.
- Priority: later: 21, none: 41.
- Closed history entries: 152.
- Surface: command: 9, flag: 39, native-command: 21, option: 72, format: 57, hook: 2, key: 93, binding: 61, native-key: 58, semantic: 58, presentation: 7, protocol: 2.

## Measured surface

The pinned oracle contains 92 commands, 78 aliases, 572 command-flag shapes (318 valueless, 246 required-value, 8 optional-value), positional minimum and maximum bounds, 180 options, 198 global formats, 153 scoped literal context pairs across 31 source producers, 10 derived context families, 36 format modifiers, 68 hooks, and 303 default bindings across 5 tables. zz has catalog entries for 83 of those commands. The registry classifies 39 catalogued-unsupported upstream flag pairs, 0 implemented flag-arity mismatches, 0 positional-minimum mismatches, 0 positional-maximum mismatches, 14 callback-bearing commands across 6 effective `args_parse` rules, 0 implemented commands without verified callback behavior, 0 zz-only flags on tmux command names, 21 native command names, 72 options absent from `BEHAVES`, 57 known limited formats, 0 scoped context-format gaps, 0 accepted-native context-format names, 2 currently documented hook-producer gaps, 93 omitted default keys, 61 divergent shared default bindings, 58 zz-only default keys.

## Enforcement boundary

The gate reconciles command names, aliases, flag arities, positional bounds, custom
`args_parse` rules, option names, global formats, scoped and derived context producers,
format modifiers, hook names,
and default key presence against the clean pinned tmux source and binary. It also reconciles
options absent from `BEHAVES`, constant-backed formats against the live registry, omitted
and zz-only default keys against zz's key tables, rendered commands plus repeat bits for
shared default bindings, the native roster against catalog minus oracle, every pinned
canonical prefix against the resolver, and known scenarios against exact tuples.

These structural checks cannot prove that runtime parsing applies each inventoried `args_parse`
rule, context-format value correctness, nonconstant format correctness, or whether a hook fires,
or that a structurally matching binding behaves identically at runtime. Differential scenarios,
attached-client fixtures, unit tests, and manual GUI checks supply that behavioral evidence. The
tracker keeps the remaining semantic discovery work explicit instead of treating matching
structure as proof.

## Gap details

### `aliases.remote-client-preflight`: Route a remote host statically to its ssh lifecycle

Settled 2026-09-02 as a native decision rather than a gap, because the clause it carried describes a zz-only surface with no pinned counterpart. The pin has no remote client at all: pinned tmux d77c9dc6 talks to one local socket, `-L` and `-S` name a path on this machine, and there is no host argument, no forwarding and nothing to preflight, so there is no pin behaviour this slug could be measured against. On the zz side the routing is a design decision the ssh-only consolidation already made: `--host` resolves statically to an ssh lifecycle, because the only forward constructor starts one, and the local CLI already does the part that has a tmux analogue, preparing a whole command vector against an already-running compatible daemon before it commits. An existing-only remote discovery pass would mean a second, connectionless probe channel over ssh purely to classify a vector, which is infrastructure zz does not want to carry for a classification step; the cost of the static route is that a `--host` vector that would have been refused locally is refused after the ssh lifecycle starts instead of before. Reopen only if zz grows a cheap existing-only remote probe for another reason and this classification can ride it.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `client`
- User impact: remote, scripts
- Items: `semantic:command-alias-remote-client-preflight`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/endpoint.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz/src/lib.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
- Acceptance:
  - `A `--host` CLI vector is classified against the static remote route and its ssh lifecycle without an existing-only discovery pass, while the local CLI keeps preparing against an already-running compatible daemon.`

### `capture.rich-transports`: Add rich capture transports

Pinned tmux backs `-R`, `-P`, `-C`, `-F`, `-H`, and `-L` with its own grid and input-parser internals: `-R` prints per-line `grid_line` flags plus every cell's colour, attribute, and hyperlink id, and `-P` drains the incomplete escape sequence still sitting in `input_pending`. zz's capture surface is the terminal worker's retained UTF-8 text snapshot, which the daemon serves for `-a`, `-e`, `-J`, `-M`, `-N`, `-p`, `-q`, `-b`, `-S`, and `-E` with the pin's routing, alongside the zz-native `show-last-output` and `capture-browser` verbs. The six rich transports stay loudly refused rather than approximated. Reopen only when a named scripted workload needs cell attributes, hyperlinks, or a saved alternate grid, which takes a terminal-owned rich snapshot rather than another text transform.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `terminal`
- User impact: scripts
- Items: `flag:capture-pane:-C`, `flag:capture-pane:-F`, `flag:capture-pane:-H`, `flag:capture-pane:-L`, `flag:capture-pane:-P`, `flag:capture-pane:-R`, `semantic:capture-pane-saved-alternate`, `semantic:capture-pane-trailing-blank-rows`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/capture-pane.txt`
- Acceptance:
  - ``capture-pane` keeps the pin's routing, boundary, and text semantics over the retained UTF-8 snapshot, and `-C`, `-F`, `-H`, `-L`, `-P`, and `-R` stay loudly unsupported rather than approximated.`
  - `The divergence matrix keeps the accepted text-snapshot shape visible, including the trailing blank viewport rows the pin emits and zz stops short of.`

### `choosers.command-flags`: Complete chooser command controls

Measured against the pin on 2026-09-01 through clients attached on real ptys, because a chooser only answers a client that is attached to it. -F is expanded once per visible row in that row's own context and becomes the text the row draws: window_tree_build_session, _window and _pane pass the product to mode_tree_add as the row text, window_buffer_build does the same in each paste-buffer's context, and the row keeps its shortcut, its order and its target. The board recipe claimed #{line} is part of that context; it is not. format_add(ft, "line") is only in window_tree_get_key and window_buffer_get_key, so #{line} belongs to -K and expands empty inside -F; measured with choose-tree -F '<L#{line}|P#{pane_id}>' on an attached 80x24 client, which drew '<L|P%0>'. zz now expands -F the same way and carries the product as ChooseTreeItem.text and ChooseBufferItem.text on protocol v93, empty when the command had no -F; the raw TUI draws it in place of the label/detail and name/size/preview row it composes otherwise, and the GPUI chooser substitutes it for the row's primary text and drops the secondary columns. -h drops the invoking pane's own row: window_tree_build_window counts the filtered panes first and skips the source only afterwards, so a window whose only match is the source keeps its row with no pane children, the session row is untouched, and a selection that would have landed on the hidden row falls to the first row while a single-pane source window keeps the window row it already started on. -k is stored on the mode entry and read by window_pane_reset_mode, which kills the pane the chooser was entered on after the mode is freed, on cancel, on Enter, on a row shortcut, and when the buffer chooser closes because its last buffer was deleted; the chosen template still runs, and it runs after the kill: window_tree_key and window_buffer_key append the template with mode_tree_run_command and then call window_pane_reset_mode, whose server_kill_pane is synchronous, so a two-pane window whose -k chooser runs `run-shell 'echo #{window_panes}'` on Enter writes 1 on the pin. zz ran the template first and wrote 2 until the cycle-6 gate reordered it; the scenario now measures that count, and a buffer paste whose source pane died falls to the client's current pane the way the pin's queued paste-buffer resolves against it. choose-buffer parses -y and never reads it, since window_buffer_init copies only -F, -K and the template and buffer-mode deletes without a prompt, so the flag is accepted and inert alone, clustered and repeated. Two divergences stay recorded rather than copied. zz's chooser is a per-client overlay and not a pane-mode stack, so a client that detaches drops the chooser instead of leaving it and the -k pane survives, which is what the pin does too because the mode outlives the detach; but raising another overlay retires zz's chooser outright where the pin keeps the mode underneath and kills the pane only when it is finally left, so a -k chooser replaced by an overlay never kills its pane in zz. While probing that, display-menu -c on a client that already has a chooser open was measured to leave the client's keys with the chooser in zz: the menu item never ran and 'q' cancelled the chooser instead, where the pin's menu takes the key and leaves the mode alone. flag:choose-tree:-y stays open: its only consumer on the pin is the PROMPT_ACCEPT that auto-answers the 'Kill session/window/pane?' prompt raised by x and X, and zz's choose-tree table delivers neither key, so accepting the flag would claim a semantic zz has no place to spend. semantic:chooser-key-vocabulary stays open with the same measurement: the pin's mode_tree_key also answers t, T, C-t, K, J, S-Up, S-Down, O, r, F1, C-h, M-- and M-+, and window_tree_key adds m, x, X and ':', none of which zz's choose-tree or choose-buffer tables name.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `medium`
- Owner: `daemon`
- User impact: daily, scripts
- Items: `flag:choose-tree:-y`, `semantic:chooser-key-vocabulary`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/render.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz/src/chooser/tree.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz/src/chooser/buffer.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `file:compat/attached-client.sh`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/chooser-row-flags.txt`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/chooser-kill-on-exit.txt`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/chooser-buffer-confirm-flag.txt`
- Acceptance:
  - `Attached-client tests cover chooser formatting, key actions, and zz-deliverable key names.`

### `clients.interactive-refresh`: Complete interactive client commands

Pinned tmux keeps mode and redraw ownership on the server: `switch-mode` installs a session and window switcher in an ordinary pane, `refresh-client` bare, `-S`, and `-c -D -L -R -U -l -r` redraw or pan a terminal client's view, and `copy-mode` sets a pane mode whether or not a client is attached. zz splits those the other way. Each client renders itself from published frames, copy and view mode live on the per-client terminal view so `MuxEffect::TerminalView` answers `pane is not attached: %N` for a clientless target, and the switcher intent is served by zz's native picker, sidebar, and chooser surfaces. Reopen only if zz gives panes a server-owned mode object or lets the daemon drive a client's drawing. Widened on 2026-09-02 for the copy-mode format family. tmux answers `pane_in_mode`, `pane_mode` and every `window_copy_formats` name off the pane's one mode entry; zz has one frozen view per client, so an aggregation rule had to be chosen rather than left unstated. zz answers the family from the daemon's single-writer `copy_sessions` map and the frozen revision each client's terminal view publishes. The aggregation rule is a zz decision, because copy mode is per client here: a pane is in mode when any client holds a non-exiting copy session on it, and the cursor, view offset and selection come from the format tree's own client when that client has a session on the pane (the `-c` client, else the invoking attached client), and from the earliest client in the mode otherwise. Measured on the pin on a throwaway server with one attached 80x24 pty client and a 300-line pane: outside a mode `pane_in_mode` answers 0, `pane_mode` and every `window_copy_formats` name answer empty; at `copy-mode` entry `pane_in_mode`=1, `pane_mode`=copy-mode, `copy_cursor_x`=0, `copy_cursor_y`=22, `scroll_position`=0, `selection_present`=0 with the four selection coordinates still empty, and `display-message -p -t %0 -c <client>` answers the same row as the bare form. `send-keys -N 5` then `-X cursor-up` moved `copy_cursor_y` 22 to 17, the next plain cursor-up 17 to 16, and `send-keys -N 4 -X` with no action then a cursor-up moved 16 to 12. After `begin-selection` and three cursor-rights `selection_start_x`=10, `selection_start_y`=385, `selection_end_x`=2, `selection_end_y`=386 and `selection_present`=1, so the start and end rows are grid rows counted from the top of the history, not screen rows, and `copy_cursor_line`=`L292 marker` with `copy_cursor_word`=`L292`. After `-X cancel` the whole family is empty again. Two consequences stay native and are not defects of that rule. `run-shell -t %0 'echo hi'` puts the pin's pane into view-mode, so `pane_mode` answers view-mode and `send-keys -t %0 -X cursor-up` answers 0 there, while zz routes run-shell output to the invoking client's overlay and opens no mode on the pane, which keeps `pane_mode` empty; and a detached `copy-mode -t <pane>` still answers `pane is not attached: %N`, so the family needs an attached client on zz where the pin needs none. Both remain semantic:copy-mode-headless-target and the run-shell routing under this group.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `client`
- User impact: remote
- Items: `command:switch-mode`, `semantic:copy-mode-headless-target`, `semantic:refresh-client-interactive`, `semantic:switch-mode-transition`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/designs/tmux-superset-roadmap.md`
  - `file:compat/attached-client.sh`
- Acceptance:
  - `Native pickers, the sidebar, and per-client copy and view mode keep the switcher and mode intent without advertising `switch-mode` or a server-driven redraw.`
  - ``refresh-client` keeps `-A -B -C -f -F -t` behaving while the interactive redraw and pan family stays loudly unsupported, and clientless `copy-mode`, `choose-tree`, and `choose-buffer` keep their attached-client errors.`
  - `The pane-scoped copy-mode formats aggregate zz's per-client mode state by a written rule rather than staying unavailable, and `run-shell -t` still opens no mode on the pane.`

### `clients.path-encoding`: Preserve non-UTF-8 client facts

The cwd half closed on 2026-09-01. Protocol v92 replaces the UTF-8-only cwd on ClientHello with ClientPath, a bounded byte string that is the client's own OsStr bytes on Unix and UTF-8 everywhere else, and registration rebuilds the exact absolute PathBuf from it. Measured before the change against a client launched in /tmp/cwd-<0xff>-dir: the pin resolved `source-file rel.conf` and zz answered `No such file or directory: rel.conf`, because the client filtered any path whose to_str() was None out of its hello. Both binaries now resolve the direct and nested relative paths, keep a quiet miss at status 0 with empty channels, and keep a loud miss at status 1 on stderr, differentially clean in smoke/client-non-utf8-cwd. Omission is unchanged where it was already correct: a relative cwd is still dropped at registration and a cwd past MAX_CLIENT_WORKING_DIRECTORY_BYTES is still omitted by the client rather than failing the connection, which the encode and decode guards still reject if one is constructed directly. Protocol v82's UTF-8 NAME=VALUE client environment is untouched and still substitutes or drops unrepresentable entries; that half needs its own byte-preserving entry shape plus the pin's update-environment byte behavior.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `medium`
- Owner: `protocol`
- User impact: scripts
- Items: `semantic:client-environment-non-utf8`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/client.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/protocol/wire-protocol.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/client-non-utf8-cwd.txt`
- Acceptance:
  - `A Unix client carries non-UTF-8 environment names and values without substitution, and selected update-environment entries retain the pin's byte behavior.`

### `clients.read-only-and-focus`: Retain native client focus semantics

Independent GUI clients and per-client sizing are core zz behavior. Widened 2026-09-02 for semantic:per-client-active-pane, relocated from clients.active-pane, which is now empty. The pin really does keep a per-client active pane: `refresh-client -f active-pane` sets CLIENT_ACTIVEPANE, `server_client_set_pane` stores the pane on a per-client-and-window record and `server_client_get_pane` reads it back, feeding target resolution through `cmd_find_from_client`, the active border and highlight in screen-redraw.c and window-border.c, `run-shell` with no target, and the tty cursor. Measured on a throwaway pin server with two attached 80x24 pty clients and a two-pane window. The flag is retained and reported: `#{client_flags}` answers `attached,focused,active-pane,UTF-8` for the client that asked and `attached,focused,UTF-8` for the other, which zz already matches. What the measurement adds is how little of it is observable in the format layer: `format_cb_pane_active` compares `ft->wp` with `wp->window->active`, so `#{pane_active}` and every `list-panes` row stay window-global on the pin too, and a per-client selection never shows up there. It is also unreachable from the CLI: `cmd_select_pane_exec` takes the per-client branch only when `cmdq_get_client(item)` has a session, and a command client's session is NULL, so `select-pane -t %1` typed on the command line moved the WINDOW's active pane from %0 to %1 and moved the rows with it even while an attached client carried the flag. Only a command running on the attached client's own queue reaches `server_client_set_pane`. The product stance: in zz the pane a window is on is model state, one shared fact that every client sees and that the snapshot carries, while per-client view state - the focused window, the client size, copy and view mode, the chooser and the sidebar - already lives on the client, which is what semantic:per-client-current-window in this group settles. Adding a per-client active pane would move a model field into the view layer and split pane targeting per client, and zz's answer is that clients differ by which WINDOW they look at, not by which pane inside it is active. The flag stays retained and reported so scripts that set it are not refused. Reopen only if zz moves the active pane out of the mux model.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `daemon`

  - `The active-pane client flag is retained and reported, while the pane a window is on stays one shared model fact that every client sees.`

### `control-mode.disconnect-cancels-command-queue`: Cancel client-owned Control queues after connection loss

zz now cancels a background if-shell or run-shell callback when its origin is gone before callback entry, but immediate hook and source replay has no connection-owned cancellation token once command execution has started. Pinned tmux frees the remaining client-owned queue on hard connection loss while letting the in-flight worker finish. The daemon reader cannot see that loss yet: crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs gives only ClientKind::Command a per-connection command worker and moves the execution context into it, while a Control client runs every request inline on the same thread that reads the socket, so a blocked command holds the reader. Giving Control the same worker means sharing the context the reader still needs for attach and session-change handling, which is the architecture half of this group. Graceful EOF follows a different drain path, re-measured against the pin on 2026-09-01 by piping three commands at once into `-C attach-session` and closing stdin: three `display-message -p` lines run all three on the pin and only the first on zz; a blocking `run-shell` first runs nothing on either; blocking second runs the first on both; blocking third runs the first two on the pin and only the first on zz. So the pin executes every queued command up to and including the first queue-yielding one and discards the rest. zz's control client truncates the pending stdin queue to a single command (retain_first_initial_stdin_before_eof in crates/zz/src/control_mode.rs), which only matches the shape where the first queued command blocks. The client cannot tell a yielding command from a fast one on its own, and a static roster of blocking verbs would be a guess, so the drain fix wants the same per-connection worker to report that a request yielded the queue. Re-confirmed 2026-09-01 from the outside while building smoke/client-resize-context: piping `refresh-client -C 120,40`, a second identical report, a `display-message -p` and `detach-client` into `-C attach-session` runs all four on the pin and only the first on zz, so that scenario had to open one Control connection per size report. Any Control differential that needs two commands down one pipe is blocked on this group. 2026-09-02, from the command-prompt -t and display-panes waits: the parked-item half of that foundation now exists in two places and is cheap. A `crossbeam_channel::Sender<()>` lives on the overlay state (CommandPrompt.waiter, DisplayPanesSession.waiter), the issuing thread installs it under the same lock that proves the overlay is still up and then blocks on the receiver, and dropping the overlay wakes it, so every exit path (answer, cancel, dismissal, timer, detach, disconnect) is covered without touching each one. An answer hands the sender out to the outcome so it is released only after the answer's own commands have run, which is cmdq_insert_after before cmdq_continue. What that mechanism does NOT give either of these groups is the other half: it parks a whole daemon thread, so it is only installed for Command and Control invokers and never for an Interactive one, whose commands run on the very thread that would deliver the key that unparks them. A per-connection worker that can yield a request and resume it later is still what both of these need, and neither the prompt nor the display-panes wait moves toward it. That foundation also owns the display-panes template residue recorded at the 2026-09-02 gate: on the pin cmd_display_panes_key inserts the chosen template after the issuing item with cmdq_get_state(item), so the template's printed output and error status reach the command client that raised the overlay (`display-panes -t C -d 0 'display-message -p "chose %%%"'` prints `chose %1` on that CLI's stdout, and a failing template exits 1 with its message on that CLI's stderr), while zz runs the template in the interactive client's context and the CLI sees nothing at exit 0. Routing a chosen template's output and status back to the parked invoker needs the same per-connection worker.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `hard`
- Owner: `daemon`
- User impact: scripts
- Items: `semantic:control-mode-disconnect-cancels-command-queue`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz/src/control_mode.rs`
  - `file:crates/zz/tests/cli_binary.rs`
  - `resource:third_party/tmux-reference/UPSTREAM.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/references/tmux-upstream.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/protocol/wire-protocol.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
- Acceptance:
  - `Killing the originating Control connection while an immediate hook or sourced command is blocked lets the already-started worker finish, cancels every later client-owned source command, hook command, and mutation, and delivers none of the canceled queue's output to a replacement Control client.`
  - `Graceful EOF and a blank Return keep the originating queue alive until its pending hook and source work drains, preserving output and retained status before the client exits.`

### `copy-mode.action-fidelity`: Complete the copy-mode action vocabulary

crates/zz-mux/src/copy_actions.rs owns all 95 pinned window-copy names with a behavior category and the pin read-only bit, and derives support from the send-keys -X parser, so 88 mapped and 7 missing names stay in step with the code. The selection lifecycle is complete: selection-mode carries the pin selflag with its abbreviations and silent no-op, stop-selection is typed apart from clear-selection, and begin-selection, other-end, select-word, and select-line arm or reset the unit the pin does. Word and line extension is measured against the pin through a throwaway-server copy-mode probe: a whitespace cursor resolves outward to the neighboring word and select-word lands on the last cell of its word. Cursor geometry, logical-line and mode-key behavior, jump/page/prompt actions, and copy formatting keep their own items. The scroll-exit latch is now writable in the mode: scroll-exit-on, -off, and -toggle rearm the bit `copy-mode -e` latches at entry, page-down-and-cancel and halfpage-down-and-cancel force the exit for one action, scroll-down-and-cancel exits on reaching the bottom whether or not a selection is live, and cursor-down-and-cancel exits only when a whole counted run stayed stuck. previous-matching-bracket completes the jump, page, and prompt family: it scans back to the nearest closing bracket inside the logical line and walks its depth back to the opener, leaving the cursor alone when there is none, which is the pinned emacs branch widened from one column to the whole line. View-relative geometry is typed too: cursor-centre-vertical and cursor-centre-horizontal park the cursor mid-view and mid-row without moving the view, scroll-top, scroll-middle, and scroll-bottom move the view so the cursor line lands on that screen row and do nothing at all when the retained revision cannot reach it, and toggle-position flips the published position readout. recentre-top-bottom completes the category: it cycles the cursor line through the middle, top, and bottom of the view, restarts at the middle whenever the cursor changed line since the last press, and clamps to what the revision allows instead of refusing like the scroll placements. goto-line is measured against the pin and rebuilt: window_copy_goto_line reads its argument with strtonum over the whole string, base ten, one optional sign and leading whitespace allowed, in the inclusive range -1 through INT_MAX, and with line numbers off the number is a scrollback offset counted from the bottom, clamped to the retained history, that moves the view and leaves the cursor on the screen row it already had. Probed on a throwaway server against forty lines in a six-row pane, `#{scroll_position}` answered 5, 7, 9, 41 and 41 for 5, 7, 9, 2147483647 and a value past the history, 6 for '+6' and 13 for ' 13', while 'abc', '3x', '0x10', '2147483648' and the empty string each left the previous position untouched, and `#{copy_cursor_y}` stayed on 5 throughout. zz now parses the same grammar and moves the same viewport instead of parking the cursor on an absolute row, and a rejected argument still reaches the mode rather than being swallowed by the mux: outside a mode `send-keys -X goto-line abc` now fails on both engines, which is the pin's order of checks. Targets past INT_MAX are not pinned targets, so the terminal treats them as no move at all. logical-line and mode-key behavior is the remaining measurement: zz's copy engine takes no mode-keys input at all, while the pin branches on it in eleven places in window-copy.c, at window_copy_key_table, window_copy_cmd_next_matching_bracket, window_copy_command, window_copy_search, window_copy_cursor_limit, window_copy_cursor_right, window_copy_cursor_up, window_copy_cursor_down, window_copy_cursor_jump_to_back, window_copy_cursor_next_word_end_pos and window_copy_cursor_next_word_end. Six of those are the same `onemore` rule, that emacs may park the cursor one column past the last cell and vi may not, and the rest change the search table, the bracket search origin, the scroll-only cursor row, and where next-word-end lands. Reaching them means the option has to travel to the copy session and the revision has to answer questions the engine currently answers with a single geometry, so this item is a plumbing item, not a per-action one. semantic:copy-mode-copy-format-and-destination closed on 2026-09-02 with the last four copy names. window_copy_do_copy_line is window_copy_do_copy_end_of_line with two lines in front of it: selflag back to SEL_CHAR and the cursor to the start of its logical line, both of which grid_reader walks with wrapping on, so the selection it builds is the whole logical line however the row wraps. zz's engine gained CopyLine with its own count policy, built from the same mode_logical_line_bounds select-line uses, and the mux maps all four names onto it through the -C and -P grammar the other copy names already carry. Measured on the pin on 2026-09-02 with an attached pty client and a pane holding `alpha beta gamma`, `delta epsilon` and `zeta`: with the cursor two columns into the middle line, `send-keys -X copy-line` put `delta epsilon` in the buffer, left `#{copy_cursor_x}` and `#{copy_cursor_y}` where they were, answered `#{selection_present}`=0 and stayed in the mode; `-N 2 -X copy-line` copied `delta epsilon` and `zeta`; `-X copy-pipe-line 'cat >file'` wrote `delta epsilon` to the file and stayed in the mode; `-X copy-pipe-line-and-cancel` and `-X copy-line-and-cancel` each copied and then left the mode; and after `send-keys -N 5` a `send-keys -X bogus-action` left the next `-X cursor-up` moving one row, not five. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-copy-line.txt asserts those thirteen facts on both binaries; it compares the cursor column against itself rather than against a number, because the pin's cursor-up keeps a desired column that zz's does not, which is the cursor-geometry item, not this one. The 95-name inventory now has seven names left, all under Vocabulary: scroll-to-mouse and the six search entry points.

- Decision: `adopt`

  - `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/session.rs`

### `copy-mode.command-fidelity`: Complete copy-mode command fidelity

Copy mode lives per client in zz and needs explicit target-client semantics. semantic:copy-mode-command-errors closed on 2026-09-01 and did not need that prerequisite: cmd_send_keys_exec runs one guard of its own before it hands the arguments to wme->mode->command, `if (wme == NULL || wme->mode->command == NULL) cmdq_error("not in a mode")` at status 1, and it covers every -X spelling. Measured on the pin: `send-keys -X cursor-up`, a bare `send-keys -X`, `send-keys -N 3 -X`, `send-keys -X bogus-action`, and `send-keys -X begin-selection` all answer `not in a mode` at status 1 on a pane with no mode entry, a bare `send-keys -N 3` answers 0 because the guard only runs under -X, every one of those -X spellings answers 0 once the pane is in copy mode, and the first form answers `not in a mode` again after `send-keys -X cancel`. zz now carries a require_mode bit on the two effects send-keys -X produces and the daemon refuses the command when no client holds a non-exiting copy session on the pane and the caller is not reading a command output, which is where zz keeps the pin's per-pane mode entry. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-command-errors.txt runs those 36 checks against one attached pty client and both binaries answer identically. The pin's guard also passes for view mode: `run-shell -t %0 'echo hi'` puts %0 into view-mode (pane_mode=view-mode, pane_in_mode=1) and `send-keys -t %0 -X cursor-up` then answers 0, while zz opens no mode on the pane for run-shell -t and answers `not in a mode` at status 1; that is run-shell -t output routing under clients.interactive-refresh, where copy and view mode live on the per-client terminal view, not the guard. semantic:copy-mode-command-counts closed on 2026-09-02. zz answers the family from the daemon's single-writer `copy_sessions` map and the frozen revision each client's terminal view publishes. The aggregation rule is a zz decision, because copy mode is per client here: a pane is in mode when any client holds a non-exiting copy session on it, and the cursor, view offset and selection come from the format tree's own client when that client has a session on the pane (the `-c` client, else the invoking attached client), and from the earliest client in the mode otherwise. Measured on the pin on a throwaway server with one attached 80x24 pty client and a 300-line pane: outside a mode `pane_in_mode` answers 0, `pane_mode` and every `window_copy_formats` name answer empty; at `copy-mode` entry `pane_in_mode`=1, `pane_mode`=copy-mode, `copy_cursor_x`=0, `copy_cursor_y`=22, `scroll_position`=0, `selection_present`=0 with the four selection coordinates still empty, and `display-message -p -t %0 -c <client>` answers the same row as the bare form. `send-keys -N 5` then `-X cursor-up` moved `copy_cursor_y` 22 to 17, the next plain cursor-up 17 to 16, and `send-keys -N 4 -X` with no action then a cursor-up moved 16 to 12. After `begin-selection` and three cursor-rights `selection_start_x`=10, `selection_start_y`=385, `selection_end_x`=2, `selection_end_y`=386 and `selection_present`=1, so the start and end rows are grid rows counted from the top of the history, not screen rows, and `copy_cursor_line`=`L292 marker` with `copy_cursor_word`=`L292`. After `-X cancel` the whole family is empty again. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-formats.txt proves the counts on both binaries by the rows each cursor-up moved, which is geometry-free. The acceptance clause was re-scoped at that close: it had asked for the counts without saying what could observe them, and the earlier reading that the attached client's own screen was the observable did not survive the measurement, because the pin renders an 80 by 23 pane while zz renders the pane inside its sidebar layout, so no marker line is common to both. The two remaining items are still gated on the dependency. A detached `copy-mode -t <pane>` from the zz CLI answers `pane is not attached: %0` and exits 1 for every spelling, plain, -q, -e, -H, -u and -d alike, while the pin enters the mode and exits 0, so no differential can reach the initial scroll position or the counts. That is `semantic:copy-mode-headless-target` under `clients.interactive-refresh`. -k is measured. cmd-copy-mode.c accepts it and never reads it there: window.c line 1417 stores it on the mode entry as wme->kill and window_pane_reset_mode kills the pane after the mode is torn down, and window_pane_set_mode returns before that assignment when the pane is already in the same mode, so re-entering copy mode never updates the bit. Probed on a throwaway server, `copy-mode -k -t %0` followed by `send-keys -t %0 -X cancel` removed %0 from list-panes, the same pair without -k left the pane alone, and -k followed by `copy-mode -q` on the last pane took the window, session and server with it. zz rejects it loudly today, `unsupported command: copy-mode -k`, which also makes `bind-key ... copy-mode -k` fail where the pin binds it. Carrying the bit does not need the wire: MuxEffect is daemon-local, so the kill can ride an effect and land on the daemon's CopySession. What it does need is a kill that runs when the copy session actually ends, which in zz is reconcile_copy_session seeing the terminal publish live mode again, and that function has no path back into the mux to remove a pane. -s is the view of another pane: window_pane_set_mode(wp, swp, ...) clones the source pane's screen into the target pane's copy backing, and window_copy_refresh_start refuses the live re-sync whenever wme->swp differs from wme->wp. zz builds its copy revision from the pane's own terminal, so -s needs a revision sourced from a second pane before the flag can be honest. flag:copy-mode:-k closed on 2026-09-02. The bit rides MuxEffect::ArmCopyModeKill, which is daemon-local, onto the daemon's CopySession, and the kill runs where window_pane_reset_mode runs it: reconcile_copy_session seeing the terminal publish live mode again queues the pane, and the queue is drained outside the state lock through the same `kill-pane` command path the exited-terminal reaper uses, so the last pane still takes its window, session and server. The effect is emitted before the entry and refuses to arm a pane already in the mode, which is window_pane_set_mode's early return, and a re-entry into an armed mode keeps the bit rather than clearing it. Re-measured against the pin on 2026-09-02 with an attached pty client and four panes: a plain `copy-mode` then `-X cancel` left every pane, `copy-mode -k` then cancel removed the target, `-k` followed by a plain `copy-mode` still removed it, a plain `copy-mode` followed by `-k` did not, `-k` then `copy-mode -q` on the last pane took the session, and `bind-key -T root F9 copy-mode -k` bound and rendered as `copy-mode -k`. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-kill-on-exit.txt runs those eight checks on both binaries; it compares pane indexes rather than ids because the harness server's own panes shift the numbering, and it kills the last pane of the test session rather than of a detached one, because a detached `copy-mode -t` still answers `pane is not attached` on zz. flag:copy-mode:-s was not attempted in that pass. zz builds the copy revision from the target pane's own terminal inside that pane's worker thread, so -s needs a revision captured on the source pane's worker and handed to the target view, which is a new synchronous terminal command plus a view action carrying the captured revision; nothing smaller was found.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `hard`
- Owner: `client`
- User impact: daily, remote
- Items: `flag:copy-mode:-s`
- Depends on: `clients.interactive-refresh`
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `file:compat/attached-client.sh`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-command-errors.txt`
  - `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/copy-mode-command-errors.sh`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-formats.txt`
  - `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/copy-mode-formats.sh`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-kill-on-exit.txt`
  - `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/copy-mode-kill-on-exit.sh`
- Acceptance:
  - `Attached-client tests cover source pane, initial scroll position, and command counts; the counts are proved through the copy-mode format family rather than through a client's rendered screen.`

### `display-message.verbose-trace`: Trace display-message format expansion

The shared expander returns only the final string and has no structured trace sink for nested conditions, modifiers, lookups, and replacements. Measured on the pin on 2026-09-02 and NOT implemented; what follows is the whole grammar so the next attempt starts from a shape rather than a reading. The trace goes to the command's stdout ahead of the result, through cmdq_print, and is emitted whether or not -p was given, because format_log1 gates only on `ft->item != NULL` and FORMAT_VERBOSE. Every line is `#`, then `es->loop` spaces, then the message: `format_log1` writes `"#%.*s%s"` with es->loop taken from a ten-space literal. es->loop is incremented by format_expand1 on entry, so a top-level expansion logs at one space and each nested expansion adds one. Mapped onto zz's expander, whose top call is `expand(format, 0)`: `expand` logs at `depth + 1` and `expand_replacement`, which is called with `depth + 1`, logs at its own `depth`, so the two agree by construction. Measured output for `display-message -v -p '#{?pane_dead,dead,#{=3:pane_title}}'` on a pane titled macbook, in order: `expanding format:` the whole string, `found #{}:` the body, `condition is: pane_dead`, `condition 'pane_dead' found: 0`, `condition 'pane_dead' is false`, `no condition matched in '...'; using last arg`, then at one more space `expanding format: #{=3:pane_title}`, `found #{}: =3:pane_title`, then at two more `expanding format: 3` and `result is: 3`, then back out `modifier 0 is =`, `modifier 0 argument 0: 3`, `format 'pane_title' found: macbook`, `applied length limit 3: mac`, `replaced '=3:pane_title' with 'mac'`, `result is: mac`, `replaced '?pane_dead,dead,#{=3:pane_title}' with 'mac'`, `result is: mac`. About 45 distinct messages exist in format.c: the expand frame (reached loop limit, expanding format, format is too long, after time expanded, result is), the finds (found #{}, found #(), #() is disabled), the modifier pair (modifier N is X, modifier N argument M), the lookup pair (format 'X' found, format 'X' not found, expanding inner format), the conditional six, the comparison three, the post-transforms (substitute, applied length limit twice, applied padding width twice, replacing with length, replacing with width), the tail (replaced 'X' with 'Y', failed X), the loop family (session, window, pane, option, environment and client loops plus their missing-context lines), the expression family of seven, plus literal string, the two content-search lines, the repeat syntax error and the time limit. Two structural mismatches were found while placing them, and both are work beyond a sink. First, the pin expands each modifier ARGUMENT through format_expand1 while it parses the modifiers, which is where the `expanding format: 3` and `result is: 3` pair at depth three comes from, and it logs the modifier only after the whole list is parsed; zz's `build_modifiers` collects arguments as raw slices and expands them later and only for the modifiers that need it, so a faithful trace needs the expansion moved into parsing, which is a behaviour change and not only a logging one. Second, zz's expander has no counterpart for the pin's `format_check_time` limit or its strftime overflow, so `reached time limit` and `format is too long` have no site to hang on. A third divergence turned up beside them and is not this group's: with a user option @three=3, the pin answers `#{=#{@three}:pane_title}` as the literal `=3:pane_title` because the modifier parse fails on a non-numeric argument and the body is then expanded as a plain format, and `#{s/#{@three}/X/:pane_title}` as the untouched title, while zz answers the plain title for both; that is modifier-argument grammar and belongs with formats.modifier-fidelity. Estimated well past this batch's budget once the argument-expansion move and the ordering tail are counted, so both items stay open with the grammar, the indentation rule and the two mismatches written down; the acceptance now names the corpus a differential should carry.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `hard`
- Owner: `mux`
- User impact: scripts, admin

### `display-popup.behavior-fidelity`: Match remaining display-popup behavior

A popup now keeps the origin and size it asked for and rebuilds the live box from them on every owning-client resize the way popup_resize_cb does — the smaller of the request and the viewport, the preferred origin whenever it fits, the clamped one when it does not, and the job resized to the content box — proved against the pin in scenario display-popup-resize-lifecycle. Style refresh, native popup pointer affordances, popup-to-pane conversion, and popup Kitty image presentation remain separate cross-daemon and client fidelity work. Real mouse and status format facts remain under formats.mouse-context; Control presentation and read-only popup policy keep their existing native-client owners. Style refresh closed on 2026-09-01 against clients attached on real ptys on both binaries (scenario display-popup-style-refresh). popup_draw_cb calls popup_reapply_styles on every draw, so popup-style and popup-border-style are read again each time from the current window of the session the popup's own client is attached to, and options_push_changes redraws every client with a session after any set-option, so a style set while the popup is up repaints the live box with no key pressed; measured with popup-border-style moved from fg=colour129 to fg=colour118 on an attached 80x24 client. popup-border-lines is copied into the popup when it is created and never read again, so setting it to double while the popup was up left the box on its single-line glyphs. The command line -s and -S styles are folded in the same way display-menu folds -s: style_parse is read back for its foreground and background alone, so an option attribute survives a -S that names none and an option colour is dropped when the command line names only the other half, and a style style_parse rejects is dropped whole. One measured residue stays open: popup_reapply_styles then zeroes the attribute field of both popup cells (pd->defaults.attr = 0 and pd->border_cell.attr = 0), so popup-style 'bold,bg=colour18,fg=colour190' drew ESC[38;5;190m ESC[48;5;18m with no ESC[1m on the pin, while zz carries the attribute to the client and draws it; that belongs with the non-colour half of the appearance bridge rather than with this refresh. semantic:display-popup-border-drag, semantic:display-popup-context-menu, semantic:display-popup-kitty-images and semantic:display-popup-to-pane stay open: the pin drives all three of the first from popup_key_cb's mouse arm, where button 3 on the left or top border (or anywhere outside the popup) raises the popup_menu_items menu (Close/Paste/Fill Space/Centre/To Horizontal Pane/To Vertical Pane), a meta drag or a drag started on a border moves the popup with button 1 and resizes it with button 3, and popup_make_pane hands the popup's job and screen to a freshly split pane through job_transfer. semantic:display-popup-kitty-images has no oracle to copy: the pinned tree has no kitty graphics support anywhere in its source and popup.c carries no ENABLE_SIXEL branch either, so popup content goes through input_parse_screen into the popup's own screen with no image path at all. The item is a zz-native presentation contract for the popup terminal rather than a fidelity gap, and closing it means proving zz's own rendering, replacement and close cleanup on an attached client. For paste, popup_key_cb answers a KEYC_PASTE_START with 0 whenever the popup has a job, so the bytes in the event buffer are dropped rather than written to the job, and a POPUP_CLOSEANYKEY popup explicitly excludes paste and mouse keys from the any-key close. semantic:display-popup-kitty-images stays open pending an attached-client proof. The pin measurement that makes this a zz contract rather than a fidelity gap was taken again at d77c9dc6: the string `kitty` appears zero times across every .c and .h file in the pinned tree, popup.c contains no image, sixel or SIXEL reference at all, and the built tree's config.h never defines ENABLE_SIXEL, so even the sixel branches guarded by it are compiled out of the pinned binary. Popup content reaches the popup's own screen through input_parse_screen with no image path anywhere, so there is nothing to copy and the item is zz's own presentation contract for a popup terminal. That contract was missing: the raw TUI's Renderer::reconcile_kitty_images walks model.layout.panes, which is the workspace layout, and a popup's pane is not in it, while every popup paint called KittyBridge::suspend first, so an image written inside a popup was placed by nobody and the popup drew its cells with the image missing. The renderer now reconciles the popup's own pane after painting it, against the content box inside the border, on both the full paint and the popup fast path in paint_frames, gated so a menu or confirm overlay above the popup suppresses it; the suspend before it has already retired every workspace placement, so the popup's images are the only ones on screen while it is up. Replacement and close cleanup fall out of the bridge that already had them. That code and its renderer test (an image placed inside a popup anchored at the content origin inside the border, replaced with the retired placement's delete, then the popup dropped with the delete and no new placement) stay in. What is still owed is the attached-client half of the acceptance, which is why the item is NOT closed: the kitty bridge only enables itself after a graphics probe the terminal has to answer, and the harness drives its clients on a plain pty that answers nothing, but that is a fixture limitation and not a structural one - pty-drive.py already reads and writes its master, so a fixture that answers the graphics query, prints an image inside the popup, asserts the `\x1b_Ga=p` placement escape at the content origin and the delete on close would prove it through a real attached client. The renderer test drives reconcile_popup_kitty_images directly and never goes through paint/paint_frames or the menu/confirm gate, so the two new call sites are exercised by nothing until that fixture lands. semantic:display-popup-context-menu, semantic:display-popup-border-drag and semantic:display-popup-to-pane stay open and still need the nested case first: any_overlay_present refuses a second overlay per client, so a menu cannot be raised over a popup at all in zz today, which is the prerequisite popup_key_cb's button-3 arm needs.

### `formats.context-producer-fidelity`: Implement missing context-format producers

Schema 5 classified 41 missing literal producer/name tuples plus current_file and next_@* or prev_@* derived families. Five slices are done. The option loop moved 24 tuples: format_loop_add_option and format_loop_add_array_item each own all twelve option-loop formats. format_loop_clients owns loop_index and loop_last_flag, counting its sorted roster from zero and flagging the final row under every order form. format_loop_environ owns environ_name, environ_value, environ_hidden, environ_removed, loop_index, and loop_last_flag for whichever store its flag word names. Both derived families are gone from the missing partition: current_file is the resolved load path of the file that queued the command, whatever queued it and however deeply nested, and each W row copies the user options stored directly on the window beside it in the sorted list under next_ and prev_, never a same-named value it merely inherits from the global, session, or pane object, with the edges and absent names expanding empty. Nine literal tuples remain, all notify_monitor_cb, and the slug stays until that producer lands. It is not a formats slice. In the pin the nine names exist only while a format monitor fires: cmd_set_option.c routes `set-hook -B name:what:format` into notify_monitor_add, monitor.c parses the middle field as `%*` for all panes, `%N` for one pane, `@*` for all windows, `@N` for one window, and anything else for the session, then arms a one-second event timer that re-expands the format per subscribed object and calls back only when the value changed. Measured against the pin on a throwaway server, `set-hook -B '@watch:@*:#{window_name}'` bound to a command that records the nine names fired nothing when it armed, because notify_monitor_add passes no MONITOR_NOTIFY_INITIAL and the first sample only records the baseline, and fired once after a rename-window with hook=@watch, hook_value=renamed, hook_last=bash, hook_window=@0, hook_window_name=renamed, hook_window_index=0, hook_session=$0, hook_session_name=mon, and an empty hook_pane for a window-scoped monitor. zz answers `invalid flag -B` and exits 1, so no monitor exists to produce that context; the nine names expand empty outside a hook, which is what the pin also does. Closing this needs the monitor subsystem in the daemon and the -B grammar in the command layer, not a producer table entry.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`

### `formats.expansion-budgets`: Keep format expansion deterministic

The output clamp is fixed and the wall clock is refused, each on its own evidence. zz used to clamp every finished expansion to MAX_STATUS_TEXT_BYTES, 4096, in truncate_output at all three entry points, so command-facing output lost everything past 4096 bytes while `#{n:}` of the same format already reported the full length. truncate_output is gone and the bound moved to where StatusLine enforces it: clamp_status_text bounds the status title, base style, and every status row as that message is built, beside the left and right sides wrap_status_style already bounded. The strict format-expansion-output-clamp differential now agrees with the pin at 9000 bytes for `#{p-9000:#{l:tail}}`, 10000 for `#{R:x,10000}`, and 6000 for a 6000-byte user option read back through `#{E:}`. The pin's FORMAT_TIME_LIMIT, 100 milliseconds of wall clock checked on entry to format_expand1, is deliberately not adopted. It was implemented and measured first: threading one FormatBudget from each entry point into every nested expander reproduced the pin's shape exactly, where after `#{n:#{Ogs:#{Ogs:#{l:x}}}}` burns the budget the trailing literal text still lands, `#{l:lit}` still expands because format_unescape only samples the clock every 10000 characters, and `#{?session_name,yes,no}` beside them expands empty. It also made zz's own command semantics load-sensitive, because zz expands option values and command arguments through the same engine and an emptied argument is a failure rather than a truncation: with the budget in place, daemon::tests::attached_client_extents_clamp_retained_and_default_dimensions failed 5 of 5 runs under eight spinning cores and passed 6 of 6 idle, while the same test on the same load passed 3 of 3 without it, and forcing the budget spent failed it deterministically inside a `set-option status off`. Trading a benign output difference for a load-dependent command failure is the wrong side of that bargain, so the divergence is accepted: a runaway expansion in zz runs to completion and answers the same string every time, where the pin returns a truncated result whose length moves run to run. FORMAT_LOOP_LIMIT was never part of this and did not change: measured on the pin, 200 sibling `#{l:x}` replacements all expand, 99 nested `#{s/x/x/:}` wrappers still reach their body, and the hundredth answers empty, which is a recursion depth of 100 on both sides rather than a running total. The 40,960,000-byte repeat ceiling stays the allocation guard it always was.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `mux`
- User impact: scripts
- Items: `semantic:format-expansion-time-budget`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/formats.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/status.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/format-expansion-output-clamp.txt`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/status-line.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
- Acceptance:
  - `Command-facing expansion stays uncapped and MAX_STATUS_TEXT_BYTES stays on the status-line message that owns it, with a differential proving both sides print the whole string.`
  - `The engine stays deterministic with no wall-clock abort, and the recursion depth guard keeps matching the pin at 100 on both sides.`

### `formats.modifier-fidelity`: Implement remaining format modifiers

The post-10v rerank split the six-token umbrella and selected R separately. w, O, L, and V are done; only I is left, and its grammar and semantics are now measured rather than forecast. The modifier takes one flag word, so `#{I/f:RGB}` asks whether the client has a terminal feature, `#{I/c:smcup}` whether it has a terminfo capability, and `#{I/e:FOO}` reads a client environment variable. Measured against the pin on a throwaway server with an 80 by 24 pty client and FOO set in its environment: `I/f:RGB` and `I/f:256` answer 1, `I/f:nosuchfeature` answers 0, `I/c:smcup` and `I/c:colors` answer 1, `I/c:nosuchcap` answers 0, `I/e:FOO` answers barvalue, and an unset name answers empty. With no client attached every form answers empty, which is format_replace's early exit on a null ft->c, a null tty term, or CLIENT_UNATTACHEDFLAGS. The body is a literal name, not a format: `#{I/c:#{l:smcup}}` answers 0 where `#{I/c:smcup}` answers 1. Flags combine last-wins in source order c, then f, then e, so `#{I/cf:RGB}` answers the feature and `#{I/cfe:FOO}` and `#{I/fe:FOO}` both answer the environment value. A missing or unknown flag word takes the parser's `argc < 1` break and falls back to a plain lookup, so `#{I:HOME}` answers the environment fallback that `#{HOME}` already answers and `#{I/z:RGB}` answers empty. Two of the three flags are backed today: zz's daemon already publishes the client's feature list through client_termfeatures and its environment store through the same rows `#{Vc:}` walks. The c flag is the blocker. tty_term_has_name reads the per-client terminfo table tmux builds for the client's TERM, and zz's daemon drives no client terminal through terminfo and loads no terminfo database, which is the same fact that already parks `messages.tty-model`. Answering 0 for every capability would make `#{I/c:smcup}` disagree with the pin on the common case while looking like support, so I stays open until either a terminfo source reaches the daemon or the c flag is parked on its own. Re-measured on 2026-09-02 with an attached 80x24 pty client on TERM=xterm-256color and FOO=barvalue, which sharpens the blocker rather than clearing it. The grammar answers held: `I/f:RGB`=1, `I/f:256`=1, `I/f:nosuchfeature`=0, `I/c:smcup`=1, `I/c:colors`=1, `I/c:nosuchcap`=0, `I/e:FOO`=barvalue, `I/e:NOSUCHVAR` empty, every form empty with no client, `I/c:#{l:smcup}`=0 against `I/c:smcup`=1, `I/cf:RGB`=1, `I/cfe:FOO` and `I/fe:FOO` and `I/ec:FOO` all barvalue because format_replace runs the three checks in the fixed source order c, f, e and each overwrites the value, `I:HOME` the environment fallback and `I/z:RGB` empty, and an empty body answers 0 under both `I/f:` and `I/c:`. What the measurement adds is that neither flag can be answered from a compiled terminfo entry alone. `I/c:AX` and `I/c:Ss` both answer 1 on this box while `term`'s reader of the same /usr/share/terminfo entry finds neither, because tty_term_has_name walks tmux's own tty_term_codes table, whose 250-odd names include tmux extensions that reach the term through ncurses extended capability reads and through tty_apply_features, which writes a feature's capability list into the term object at connect time; `I/c:Sync` answers 0, so it is not simply true for every extension. And the f flag is not the published feature list either: zz would answer `I/f:256`=0 from client_termfeatures, which on the pin reads bpaste,ccolour,clipboard,cstyle,focus,RGB,title with no 256 in it, while the pin answers 1 because tty_feature_present falls back to checking that every capability the named feature lists is present on the term. So the c flag needs tty_term_codes plus the extended-capability reads, and the f flag needs the tty_features table beside it; a plain terminfo lookup would disagree with the pin on the common case while looking like support, which is the same reason this item stayed open before. Re-measured again on 2026-09-02 and NOT implemented; the blocker is now decomposed far enough that the next attempt is mechanical, and the reason records the shape rather than a forecast. `I/c:` is answerable from three sources and only one of them is the compiled entry. `infocmp -x xterm-256color` on this box lists 248 capabilities and DOES carry `AX` in its extended section, so `I/c:AX`=1 needs the extended read and nothing else; the `term` crate's reader missing it is a reader limitation, not a tmux extension. `Ss` is NOT in that entry, only `Se=\E[2 q` is, so `I/c:Ss`=1 comes from `tty_apply_features` writing the `cstyle` feature's capability list into the term, and `cstyle` is on because the default `terminal-features` option is `xterm*:clipboard:ccolour:cstyle:focus:title` and `tty_term_create` matches it with fnmatch against the term name. `Sync` is in neither, and no enabled feature writes it, which is why it answers 0. So `present(cap)` is `cap in the extended terminfo entry` OR `cap in the capability list of an enabled feature`, and `tty_term_has_name` is that predicate restricted to the ~250 names in tty_term_codes. The enabled set for a plain pty client is exactly three sources, all portable: the terminal-features option matched by fnmatch on the term name, the COLORTERM environment check (truecolor or 24bit adds RGB, a value containing 256 adds 256), and tty_term_create's own detection, which adds `bpaste,focus,title` when the term has the XT flag or its clear starts with CSI and adds `RGB` when it has Tc or RGB without setrgbf and setrgbb. tty_default_features is keyed on the terminal PROGRAM name (mintty, tmux, iTerm2, foot, WezTerm and friends), not on TERM, so it contributes nothing to a bare pty client, which is why the pin's list for one is exactly bpaste,ccolour,clipboard,cstyle,focus,RGB,title: five from the option and two from detection. A second blocker that was not recorded before: zz's own published feature list is not the pin's and cannot be the source. On the same pty with TERM=xterm-256color the pin answers `client_termfeatures=bpaste,ccolour,clipboard,cstyle,focus,RGB,title` (7 names) while zz answers `256,bpaste,ccolour,clipboard,hyperlinks,cstyle,extkeys,focus,mouse,osc7,overline,RGB,strikethrough,sync,title,usstyle` (16), because the daemon publishes what its own renderers support rather than what the client's terminal was detected to support. Any port of tty_apply_features that took zz's list would write a different capability set into the term and answer `I/c:Sync`=1 where the pin answers 0. So the work is: port tty_term_codes (about 250 name-and-type entries), port tty_features from tty-features.c with each feature's capability list, port the fnmatch pass over terminal-features plus the COLORTERM and XT/Tc detection, read the client's TERM through `infocmp -x` once per TERM (the term crate 0.7 reader skips the extended section), and answer `I/c:` and `I/f:` from that term object rather than from client_termfeatures, which stays zz's own renderer roster. Estimated at more than the two hours this batch budgeted, so the slug stays open with the shape written down; `I/e:` and the grammar are already backed and would fall out of the same change.

### `formats.mouse-context`: Expose mouse event formats

The pin fills `mouse_x`, `mouse_y`, `mouse_pane`, `mouse_line`, `mouse_word`, `mouse_hyperlink`, `mouse_status_line`, and `mouse_status_range` only from the mouse key event that invoked a command through its mouse key tables. zz installs no tmux mouse key names at all, because pointer gestures belong to each rendering client, which is already accepted under `keys.root-native-mouse` and `keys.copy-mode-native-mouse`. No command ever runs with an originating mouse record to publish, so the eight names stay unavailable and `mouse_x` and `mouse_y` keep the pin's null. Reopen only if zz installs tmux mouse key tables, which revives `mouse.bound-context` first.

- Depends on: none

### `formats.native-typed-context-producers`: Keep typed native context producers explicit

zz deliberately replaces these tmux format-driven mode and overlay producers with typed client state and native rendering. The explicit accepted partition prevents their 54 source tuples from being mistaken for either implemented tmux contexts or unclassified omissions. Ten tuples left on 2026-09-02, all `window-copy.c:window_copy_formats`: copy_cursor_line, copy_cursor_word, copy_cursor_x, copy_cursor_y, scroll_position, selection_end_x, selection_end_y, selection_present, selection_start_x and selection_start_y are now produced by the daemon from the per-client copy session rather than replaced by native UI state, which is exactly the second clause's exit, so they moved to the mux-owned partition and the invariant fell from 54 to 44. The rest of that producer's vocabulary stays here: copy_cursor_hyperlink, copy_position, copy_position_limit, rectangle_toggle, search_count, search_count_partial, search_match, search_present, search_timed_out, selection_active, selection_mode and top_line_time are still unproduced, and search_present and search_match in particular need the pin's searchmark array, which zz's search state does not model.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `client`
- User impact: gui
- Items: `semantic:native-format-context-producers`
- Depends on: none

  - `resource:compat/tmux-oracle.json`
  - `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/compat_manifest_tests.rs`

  - `A tuple leaves this partition only when zz exposes its tmux producer rather than merely retaining equivalent typed native UI state.`

### `formats.pane-process`: Expose remaining pane process formats

Three of the four are done and the fourth is re-scoped onto what the name actually means. pane_pipe, pane_pipe_pid and pane_unseen_changes moved from constant backings to the delegated partition and are answered by the daemon, proved differentially by compat/scenarios/smoke/format-pane-process.txt on both binaries: a fresh pane answers `pipe=0 pid=[] unseen=0`, pipe-pane raises pane_pipe to 1 and pane_pipe_pid to a live pid whose argument vector still names the pipe destination, and a bare pipe-pane drops both back. pane_unseen_changes is NOT the batch's premise of "output an attached client has not seen", and that premise is what needed correcting: input.c:1039 raises PANE_UNSEENCHANGES only inside `if (!TAILQ_EMPTY(&wp->modes))`, window.c:1448 drops it when the last mode entry goes, and window-copy.c:3013 drops it again from the copy-mode refresh timer. Measured on the pin: output to a detached window with no mode leaves it 0, output while the pane is in copy mode raises it to 1, and cancelling the mode drops it. zz carries the bit on the per-client copy session record instead of on the pane, which clears on the same two edges for free because entering a mode writes a fresh record; the daemon raises it from the pane-output activity producer for every non-exiting copy session on the pane. The per-client model is visible in one place the pin's is not: with two clients in copy mode on one pane, zz raises the bit on both records and answers 1 until every session is gone, where the pin has one pane flag; the answer is the same in every single-client state. pane_pb_progress stays open with its blocker measured rather than guessed. It is not paste-buffer progress: format.c:2380 reads `ft->wp->base.progress_bar.progress`, which screen.c:342 `screen_set_progress_bar` writes and input.c:3005 `input_osc_9` parses out of the ConEmu OSC 9;4 sequence, so it is a persistent per-pane progress-bar value and not a transient one. A fresh pane answers 0 and pane_pb_state answers `hidden` on the pin, where zz answers 0 and empty, so the pair agrees only in the default state. Closing it means parsing OSC 9;4 in the VT and retaining the state and progress on the pane, which is zz-terminal work; pane_pb_state is the same family and is tracked beside it.

- Decision: `adopt`
- Status: `open`
- Priority and ease: `later` / `medium`
- Owner: `daemon`
- User impact: scripts
- Items: `format:pane_pb_progress`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/formats.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/status.rs`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/format-pane-process.txt`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
- Acceptance:
  - `pane_pipe answers 0 or 1 off the attached pipe and pane_pipe_pid declines unless a pipe is attached, otherwise naming the job process that runs the pipe command, with a differential covering attach and a bare pipe-pane detach.`
  - `pane_unseen_changes answers 1 only for output that arrives while the pane holds a mode, 0 without a mode, and 0 again after the mode is cancelled and after it is re-entered.`
  - `pane_pb_progress answers the pane's OSC 9;4 progress-bar value, which is 0 on a fresh pane and follows the sequence's progress argument.`

### `formats.pane-runtime`: Expose pane mode formats

`pane_in_mode`, `pane_mode`, `pane_key_mode` and `pane_search_string` read tmux's `window_pane` mode stack, one shared object per pane. zz's copy, view and search state lives on the per-client terminal view, where the daemon keys `copy_sessions` by client, so two clients can view one pane in different modes with different queries. The original acceptance clause said all four therefore stay unavailable. That clause was re-scoped on 2026-09-02 for the first two, because the measurement gives them a single answer that does not depend on which viewer is asked. Measured on the pin on a throwaway server with one attached 80x24 pty client and a 300-line pane: outside a mode `pane_in_mode` answers 0, `pane_mode` and every `window_copy_formats` name answer empty; at `copy-mode` entry `pane_in_mode`=1, `pane_mode`=copy-mode, `copy_cursor_x`=0, `copy_cursor_y`=22, `scroll_position`=0, `selection_present`=0 with the four selection coordinates still empty, and `display-message -p -t %0 -c <client>` answers the same row as the bare form. `send-keys -N 5` then `-X cursor-up` moved `copy_cursor_y` 22 to 17, the next plain cursor-up 17 to 16, and `send-keys -N 4 -X` with no action then a cursor-up moved 16 to 12. After `begin-selection` and three cursor-rights `selection_start_x`=10, `selection_start_y`=385, `selection_end_x`=2, `selection_end_y`=386 and `selection_present`=1, so the start and end rows are grid rows counted from the top of the history, not screen rows, and `copy_cursor_line`=`L292 marker` with `copy_cursor_word`=`L292`. After `-X cancel` the whole family is empty again. Only the mode's identity and its presence are asked of those two names, and both agree across every client in the mode on a pane, so zz answers the family from the daemon's single-writer `copy_sessions` map and the frozen revision each client's terminal view publishes. The aggregation rule is a zz decision, because copy mode is per client here: a pane is in mode when any client holds a non-exiting copy session on it, and the cursor, view offset and selection come from the format tree's own client when that client has a session on the pane (the `-c` client, else the invoking attached client), and from the earliest client in the mode otherwise. `pane_in_mode` and `pane_mode` now answer from that map and their format registrations moved from the constant-backed partition to the delegated one. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-formats.txt asserts 0 and empty outside a mode, 1 and copy-mode inside it, and empty again after cancel, identically on both binaries. `pane_key_mode` and `pane_search_string` keep the old stance: the key table and the search query genuinely differ per viewer in zz, with no measurement that collapses them, and they stay next to the accepted `buffer_mode_format`, `client_mode_format` and `tree_mode_format` in `formats.native-modes`. Reopen those two when a named workload defines how multiple viewers aggregate.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`
- Priority and ease: `none` / `none`
- Owner: `client`
- User impact: scripts, daily
- Items: `format:pane_key_mode`, `format:pane_search_string`
- Depends on: none
- Evidence:
  - `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`
  - `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/formats.rs`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`
  - `resource:knowledge/tmux/status-line.md`
  - `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-formats.txt`
- Acceptance:
  - ``pane_in_mode` and `pane_mode` answer the pin's values for a pane some client is in copy or view mode on, by the written per-client aggregation rule; `pane_key_mode` and `pane_search_string` stay unavailable rather than reporting one viewer's state as the pane's.`

### `formats.session-activity-wake-lifecycle`: Keep native wake lifecycle outside session activity

Pinned tmux refreshes session activity when a suspended tty client wakes or unlocks. zz has no suspended attached-client state or wake/unlock protocol message; reconnect and reattach already flow through the ordinary attach activity seam.

- Decision: `native`

### `formats.terminal-cells`: Expose terminal cell formats

`cursor_character`, `cursor_colour`, `pane_bg`, `pane_fg`, `pane_tabs`, and `pane_pb_state` read live cells and terminal state out of tmux's own screen, because in tmux the multiplexer is the terminal. In zz the terminal worker owns the VT and publishes frames to the clients that draw them, while the mux format engine models sessions, windows, and panes; it holds no grid and cannot sample one without reaching across the PTY drain. The six names stay in the always-unavailable set the status-line reference records. Reopen when a named scripted workload justifies a bounded terminal-fact channel that publishes cursor cell, tab stops, and progress state without blocking drain.

- Decision: `native`
- Status: `accepted`

- User impact: scripts

### `formats.terminal-runtime`: Expose terminal runtime formats

These 28 names report live VT state the pin reads straight from its own screen: cursor position and shape, alternate screen and saved cursor, DEC private modes, mouse reporting modes, history byte counts, scroll region, and graphics support. zz keeps that state in the terminal worker and the clients that draw it, so the mux publishes the pin's inactive or default value for each name, with `cursor_flag` and `wrap_flag` enabled and the rest zero, which the status-line reference already records as pinned default state rather than support. Zero is also the true answer for `sixel_support`, which zz does not implement. Reopen through the same bounded terminal-fact channel `formats.terminal-cells` names, once a workload asks for live VT facts in formats.

- Decision: `native`
