| `alerts.message-lifecycle` | 2026-08-26 | Bell, Activity, and Silence messages now use the daemon-owned ActiveClientMessage lifecycle for every eligible attached Interactive client. Each recipient owns its message identity and appends an exact `<client> message: <text>` entry to the bounded server log, where `<client>` is the registered name or `device-<id>` fallback. A positive display-time clears sticky ignore-keys, arms a cancellable deadline, freezes terminal publication, and replaces the previous message; zero duration waits for input. Stale deadlines cannot clear replacements, dismissal and expiry publish an identity-specific clear, and thaw sends the latest full viewport unless another prompt or message still freezes the client. RequestFull repair, resync, and popup publication obey the same gate, so recovery traffic cannot expose newer terminal state while the message remains active. Control clients receive pane BEL output but no alert status message or alert log entry. The attached zz/tmux fixture replaces a 1,500 ms sticky message with a 5,000 ms alert, proves 1.8 seconds of freeze across the old deadline, repeats a BEL on the same unvisited pane while the bell flag stays set, drains the pin's stale timer for 5.2 seconds, and proves zero-duration persistence and input dismissal. No protocol change was needed. zz deliberately keeps identity-safe timer cancellation instead of reproducing the pin's stale-timer bug. | `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/designs/tmux-drop-in.md`, `resource:knowledge/playbooks/compat-harness.md` |
| `alerts.remaining-edge-cases` | 2026-08-24 | The session_activity_flag and session_silence_flag formats now mirror the resolved target window, so list-sessions reads the active window and list-windows varies per row. Attach clears the mux-visible bell, activity, and silence flags only on the session's active window before producing the snapshot. Alert action gating and message labels are decided once from that active window and fan the same decision to every eligible Interactive client while the broader per-client focus model remains unchanged. Every successful monitor-silence write, including a same-value write or repeated global reset, emits MonitorSilenceChanged and resets every live window timer; a missing local -u and a rejected -o do not. Active status messages are dismissed before dispatch by a surviving bulk Text packet or explicit Paste as well as by writable key presses, while suppressed trailing text and every read-only input leave them armed. At that 2026-08-24 checkpoint, alert-produced messages still bypassed the daemon-owned lifecycle, including the pin's terminal-publication freeze; the later alerts.message-lifecycle closure supersedes that residue. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/formats.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/alerts.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/status-line.md` |
| `alerts.repeated-bell-edge` | 2026-08-26 | TerminalSession now publishes one reliable TerminalEvent::Bell for every BEL callback instead of suppressing later callbacks behind terminal state. The mux owns the visible pane and window bell flags, so repeated Bell events and messages can arrive while those flags remain set; selection or attach clears the mux-visible state. A terminal unit writes two BEL bytes and receives two events, the live daemon integration rings the same noncurrent pane twice before a visit and receives both client Bell events, and the attached zz/tmux fixture repeats the Bell on one unvisited pane while `#{window_bell_flag}` remains 1. This matches the pinned alerts.c:196-215 behavior without a protocol change. | `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/session.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:third_party/tmux-reference/UPSTREAM.md` |
| `aliases.client-preflight` | 2026-08-25 | For local default and explicit socket endpoints, the CLI now asks an already-running compatible daemon to prepare the complete argv chain once without spawning. Resolved command identity and alias-match metadata decide native attach, TUI new-session or attach, agent-send stdin capture, and kill-server recovery before any client-owned preprocessing. Exact attach and attach-session shadows stay in command mode, arbitrary aliases to attaching commands enter the TUI, aliases to agent-send read stdin, and an agent-send shadow does not. Command execution reuses the prepared connection; TUI carries the typed immutable vector across its second connection and sends prepared requests without a second alias lookup. The CLI scans every typed result before stdin capture, attach or TUI routing, and execution, so a later preparation error cannot fall through or follow an earlier effect. A bare unaliased kill-server enters verified incompatible-daemon recovery only for transport or handshake failure; alias errors and nonzero results return normally and leave the daemon alive. Raw --kill-server remains an unaliasable process selector, --restart-daemon still recovers after a failed preflight, and preparation never autospawns. The later mux.local-cli-autospawn-parse-abort closure supersedes the former spawnable missing-daemon fail-open path, and aliases.config-parse-unit later closed config replay snapshotting. Remote --host preprocessing remains open under aliases.remote-client-preflight, while replay-group parse abort remains under mux.chain-parse-abort. | `resource:crates/zz/src/lib.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/client.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/lib.rs`, `file:crates/zz/tests/cli_binary.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/control-alias-prepare.txt`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/cli-chain-parse-abort.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `aliases.command-bodies` | 2026-08-30 | User aliases now expand one immutable layer into an opaque typed command group. Multi-command bodies execute every child through the normal mux and daemon paths, caller arguments and client-owned stdin attach only to the final child, and empty bodies succeed without effects. Recursive group rendering preserves option boundaries, empty arguments, typed blocks, and physical source groups across preparation, stored commands, source replay, local CLI routing, and Control transport. Control omits a synthetic parent frame and emits inherited-flags child guards. Queue-owned failures, shutdown, source yields, structural hooks, and shell jobs retain the pin's tested ordering. The eight-step aliases-multi-body differential has zero mismatches, and focused mux, daemon, Control, CLI, and binary regressions pass. Exact forced-shutdown multi-window window-unlinked order remains open under hooks.shutdown-window-unlinked-order because tmux derives it from retained RB-tree history. The alias group itself reuses protocol v84's CommandInvocation shape. Closure review advanced protocol v85 to append typed post-admission callback provenance and daemon-authoritative Attached reconnect state; these hardening fields do not add an alias child-vector encoding. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/lib.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/control_mode.rs`, `file:crates/zz/tests/cli_binary.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/aliases-multi-body.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/playbooks/compat-harness.md` |
| `aliases.config-parse-unit` | 2026-08-29 | Config construction now stores each original invocation beside its alias-expanded command or preparation error before replay starts. The daemon parses a file, applies that file's environment assignments, and prepares all commands under one engine lock. An earlier replayed command therefore cannot change the alias used by a later same-line or later-line command from that parsed file. Startup roots finish construction before startup replay; one source-file invocation constructs every matched file before replaying the batch, while each nested source receives a fresh snapshot when its parent source command runs. Stored preparation errors remain deferred to their original replay positions, retain source and physical-group metadata, and freeze Control warning or guard classification with their error text. Four focused daemon tests cover startup roots, same-file mutation, per-file environment timing, multi-file batches, nested refresh, parse-only behavior, deferred errors, and Control diagnostics. The two-step config-alias-parse-unit differential matches the pin in every comparison channel. Eager construction errors for source-file -n remain under mux.chain-parse-abort, and empty or multi-command alias execution remains under aliases.command-bodies. No protocol change was needed. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/config-alias-parse-unit.txt`, `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/config-alias-parse-unit.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `aliases.control-prepare` | 2026-08-25 | Protocol v74 appends request-correlated PrepareCommandList and PreparedCommandList messages and a prepared bit on CommandRequest. The daemon prepares each complete input vector under one mux lock, expanding exactly one live alias layer, preserving source and caller arguments, returning canonical identity plus alias-match and typed error state, and performing no execution, target or format resolution, hook emission, message publication, or authorization. Control prepares the initial argv unit before its flags-0 result and every complete LF line before any flags-1 frame, preserves bare initial unknown errors, whole-line parse-error framing, numbering, ignored lines, queued stdin, and interleaved notifications, then executes the immutable prepared invocations without a second alias lookup. Each request carries a nonzero request id, stale replies are ignored, every ClientKind handles the RPC, and a prepared line keeps the alias snapshot even when an earlier command mutates command-alias. The prepared bit is an internal freeze marker rather than an authority token: a forged prepared request bypasses alias lookup by design but still passes the ordinary read-only authorization gate, and a destructive forged request from a read-only client is rejected without mutation. Local ordinary CLI preprocessing is closed under aliases.client-preflight; remote preprocessing remains under aliases.remote-client-preflight, and actual empty and multi-command alias bodies remain under aliases.command-bodies. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/client.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/control_mode.rs`, `file:crates/zz/tests/cli_binary.rs`, `resource:knowledge/protocol/wire-protocol.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `aliases.typed-resolution` | 2026-08-25 | MuxEngine::resolve_command_alias now returns Miss, Expanded, or MatchedUnsupported with empty, multiple-command, and unparsable reasons. Every mux and daemon construction entry point converts a matched unsupported body to `unknown command: <typed name>` instead of falling through to a shadowed canonical name or catalog alias. Direct execution, daemon routing, terminal-qualified replay, bind-key and set-hook storage, option-command normalization, and read-only preflight share that result. Stored bind-key and set-hook command lists execute the commands constructed for storage without another user-alias lookup; read-only clients authorize the same frozen list as one all-or-nothing chain before effects. Typed `if-shell`, `run-shell`, and `confirm-before` callbacks are likewise frozen after lexical construction. Typed `if-shell` and `run-shell` callbacks preserve physical groups: a failed group stops its remaining commands while later physical lines continue; string callbacks remain one group. Typed `command-prompt` templates retain their structured constructed command list through submission without another user-alias lookup. String templates substitute raw source before a fresh parse and complete construction pass against the then-current alias table. Both prompt paths replace the first `%%` and every `%1`, with trailing-percent quoting; typed substitution preserves leaf-argument boundaries and physical groups, while string templates and free input form one group. `set-hook` and command-valued native set-option intentionally apply their documented second construction stage. A selected typed `display-menu` action drops its structural wrapper before its fresh construction stage, while a quoted brace string remains literal. Alias-resolution failures use the same command-output and key-command-failed path as other per-command failures. Focused regressions prove `kill-server=`, multi-command `list-windows` and `lsw` shadows, and an unparsable `kill-session` shadow cannot shut down, list, kill a session, replace a binding, install a hook, or change an option command. Each construction path admits one user-alias layer, and a malformed shadow refuses before read-only authorization. `aliases.command-bodies` remains open for actual empty and multi-command execution; local CLI preprocessing is closed under aliases.client-preflight, while remote preprocessing remains under aliases.remote-client-preflight. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/designs/tmux-superset-roadmap.md` |
| `buffers.client-file-context` | 2026-09-01 | load-buffer and save-buffer now route their file through the client that invoked them, the way file_read and file_write do. The daemon expands the path exactly once in its own format context, a leading ~/ against the server's own home and anything else relative against server_client_get_cwd(c, NULL) — the config client during startup, an unattached client's own working directory, then that client's attached session cwd, then home. An unattached command client then does the bounded read, or the create-truncate or -a append, on its own host over the protocol v89 ClientFileRequest/ClientFileResponse pair, correlated by request id and released when the connection ends; an attached Interactive or Control client keeps daemon-side IO, as the pin keeps server-side IO for an attached client. Failures read `strerror: path` the way cmd-load-buffer.c and cmd-save-buffer.c print them, and a client-side read that fails after the open reports Input/output error, which is what the pin's bufferevent path reports. The client reports its working directory the way find_cwd does, preferring PWD when it resolves to the same place as getcwd. Eleven focused CLI tests drive a daemon whose directory and home are deliberately not the client's; the two three-step differentials run seven and nine checks per engine over relative, nested, tilde, absolute, append, truncate, and failure paths. | `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/client.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/buffer-client-file-load.txt`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/buffer-client-file-save.txt`, `resource:knowledge/protocol/wire-protocol.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `buffers.clipboard-write` | 2026-09-01 | load-buffer -w and set-buffer -w now write the stored buffer to the selected client's clipboard. The target follows the pin's CMD_CLIENT_TFLAG plus CMD_CLIENT_CANFAIL rule: -t picks an attached client, no -t picks the current one, and an unresolvable -t is quiet and still stores the buffer. As in cmd-set-buffer.c and cmd-load-buffer.c the write goes straight to that one client and never through the set-clipboard gate that guards the application OSC 52 path, so it fires with set-clipboard off and raises no pane-set-clipboard hook; -a writes the joined buffer the way the pin writes bufdata. A client that advertises no clipboard terminal feature is the pin's Ms-less client: the command succeeds, stores the buffer, and writes nothing. That mapping is wider than the pin's: zz's client_term_features reports clipboard for any colour-capable client, so in zz only a Control client is the Ms-less case, and a pty client whose terminal lacks the pin's Ms capability (a TERM=vt100 client gets no OSC 52 from the pin) would still receive the write from zz. The scenario attaches a real pty client beside a control client on both binaries and compares the decoded OSC 52 payload stream, which matched on all ten checks. zz still encodes the selection field as `c` where the pin sends the empty field, and a non-UTF-8 buffer is not written because the wire clipboard payload is a String. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/buffer-clipboard-write.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |

| `clients.detach-exec` | 2026-09-01 | detach-client -E now takes a shell command and reaches the presentation process as the protocol v91 ClientExitAction::Exec carried on the Detached event. Victim selection is unchanged, so bare, -t, -a, and -s pick exactly the clients ordinary detach picks; each pty victim is replaced instead of detached, while a Control victim is left attached and running, matching the pin, where MSG_EXEC only acts in the pty client's attached dispatch. The shell is the victim session's default-shell with the pin's _PATH_BSHELL fallback for an unusable value, and the raw-terminal client restores the terminal, then execs `shell -c command` with argv0 from the shell's basename and SHELL exported, printing no detach notice and hanging up no parent, the way client.c runs MSG_EXEC before the exit message. An empty -E is server_client_exec's early return: the victim is neither replaced nor detached. -E wins over -P. The differential attaches a real pty client to both binaries and compares the return code, the exit notice shape, whether the parent saw SIGHUP, the client process's wait status, and the surviving client count for every case; all six observations matched, including exec statuses 7 and 5. The GUI client has no process to replace and reports the command instead. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/app.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/client-exit-actions.txt` |
| `clients.detach-targeting` | 2026-08-25 | The catalog accepts detach-client -t, and one no-wire daemon resolver now serves detach-client and switch-client. Explicit targets resolve exact attached client names, native device names, full tty paths, tty paths without /dev/, and one trailing-colon variant before any -s lookup. Without a selector, an attached Interactive or Control client selects itself; a Command client selects the best client on its origin pane's session, then falls back to the best client on the most recently active attached session. detach-client -s wins over -a and quietly does nothing when its source session is missing; -a detaches every attached peer except the resolved target, while bare detach selects only that client. A read-only client may detach only itself. Requested detach publishes the existing Requested reason without a by-client, while attach-session -d eviction retains Evicted; unregister cleanup remains unchanged. Local terminal surfaces always publish a discovered tty, so switch-client and detach-client retain their full-tty and /dev/-stripped aliases even outside nested tmux; remote clients still omit the host tty. The separate client-nested-v1 hello capability is emitted only when TMUX is nonempty and gates nested refusal together with an exact pane-tty match. Focused mux and daemon tests cover parsing, selector and source precedence, origin and fallback selection, aliases, read-only authorization, reasons, victim sets, and nested-marker lifecycle. The attached-client fixture obtains each real outer PTY, targets the client by that tty, and requires the attached client count to reach zero. For nested refusal it removes whitespace from the complete pane history and fixed pin text, snapshots the count of exact normalized substrings before each attempt, requires that count to increase afterward, and holds the attached count at one through a settle for both attach-session and new-session -A. It then proves env -u TMUX admits both paths and uses the retained root tty to detach only the forced nested client. The later clients.tty-basename-targeting closure aligned every supported client-selector caller and removed the display-panes basename widening. The later clients.local-control-terminal-facts closure extended only tty identity and nested intent to a local Control client whose stdin is a terminal. detach-client -E and parent-HUP exit actions remain separate open gaps. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/client.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `file:crates/zz-mux/tests/hunt_claims.rs`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `clients.event-hooks` | 2026-08-27 | The daemon now retains the latest attached client for each window and produces client-active, client-focus-in, client-focus-out, client-resized, client-light-theme, and client-dark-theme at their retained report boundaries. client-active fires only when the window's latest client changes; repeated focus, theme, and positive Interactive size reports each fire, even when the retained value is unchanged. Focus-in precedes a resulting active event, resize follows a resulting active event, focus-out never changes the latest client, and loss of the latest client promotes the remaining client with greatest activity before client-detached. Control clients do not originate focus, theme, resize, or input-driven active events, but an attached Control client can win loss promotion. Hooks remain clientless: hook_client identifies the reporter, client notifications expose no hook-specific session variables, copied session, window, and shared active-pane state supplies ordinary target formats, and ordinary client facts keep their independent selection rules. Per-client active-pane behavior remains under clients.active-pane. Focused daemon tests cover all six names, duplicate reports, ordering, suppression, Control exclusions, and loss promotion; the real two-client fixture proves the TUI-drivable active, focus, and resize sequence against pinned tmux without a protocol change. Post-geometry format timing for changed resize reports remains under clients.event-resize-context. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/tmux_options.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/designs/tmux-superset-roadmap.md` |
| `clients.event-resize-context` | 2026-09-02 | Both re-scoped clauses are proved by compat/scenarios/smoke/client-resized-context.txt on both binaries, and the group closes without deferring anything. The report clause holds as measured: one changed Interactive outer resize raises client-resized exactly once with `#{client_width}` current and `#{window_width}x#{window_height}` equal to the geometry the resize replaced, an unchanged report of the same size raises nothing at all, and two Control `refresh-client -C 120,40` connections resize the window to 120x40 while raising neither client-resized nor client-active. The client clause needed the fix the cycle-6 revert asked for. `notify_insert_one_hook` appends a hook body with client NULL, so `cmdq_fire_command` resolves it through `cmd_find_client(item, NULL, 1)` -> `cmd_find_current_client` -> `cmd_find_best_client`, a pure activity_time comparison the MSG_RESIZE arm of server-client.c never updates; the reporting client reaches the body only as `#{hook_client}`. zz now resolves a hook body's format client the same way, through the most recently active session's activity winner rather than through the invoking client, in `format_hook_facts_for_client`. With two pty clients attached and both hooks logging `h:#{hook_client} t:#{client_tty}`, resizing the older client A logs `active h:A t:B` then `resized h:A t:B c:80x24`, and after one key in A, resizing B logs `active h:B t:A` then `resized h:B t:A c:100x30`: the body reads the other client's tty and size while hook_client names the reporter, and the client-active the resize provokes precedes the client-resized, on both binaries. Two identity facts the fixture had to work around are recorded here rather than claimed: zz names a pty client by its tty exactly as the pin does, so client_tty and hook_client are comparable, but `#{session_attached_list}` answers the device name in zz where the pin answers the tty, and the window geometry a zz hook body sees is smaller than the pin's for the same pty because the raw client reserves chrome, so the fixture compares the lag rather than the measurement. The hook body still carries no format client SESSION: `#{session_active}` stays empty in a zz hook body where the pin answers 1, because `ExecutionContext::has_no_client` forces FormatClient::NoClient; that is a separate producer question and belongs to formats.context-producer-fidelity. | `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/app.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh` |

It does not inspect terminal size, publish client-size-v1, send ClientTerminalSize, infer geometry, retain TERM or terminal-name facts, or expose its tty through ClientFormatFacts. Control geometry remains explicit refresh-client -C state. | `clients.read-only-local-view-actions` | 2026-08-25 | Read-only clients can use direct local scroll, copy-mode entry, movement, history, line, word, paragraph, prompt, bracket, goto-line, set-mark, jump-to-mark, and cancel actions. One typed copy-action classifier also authorizes `send-keys -X`; selection, copying, search, rectangle, jump capture, paste, clear-history, raw mouse, mixed wheel, and application pane-focus effects remain blocked. Direct command requests and an entire stored binding chain are preflighted before any effect, including one-layer aliases, and rejected paths report `client is read-only` without terminal or browser input. | `clients.tty-basename-targeting` | 2026-08-25 | Every implemented tmux command flag that selects an attached client now shares exact registered-name, exact displayed client_name, full-tty, and exactly one leading-/dev/-prefix removal, with exactly one optional trailing colon. The displayed-name path covers tmux's tty, registered-name, client-PID, and device-N fallback ladder, so a nameless Control client can be targeted by the same client-PID value returned from list-clients. The shared device-N alias remains, while popup, menu, confirm, refresh, and lock keep their documented numeric and client-N native aliases. A final pathname basename is not a selector: /dev/pts/3 accepts pts/3 but rejects 3 unless 3 is the exact client name. | `clients.tui-command-output-navigation` | 2026-08-26 | zz-tui now sends every command-output press and repeat through the daemon's pane-targeted Key path. Release events remain inert, and the output surface owns mouse and paste input instead of leaking either to the hidden workspace. The daemon resolves the target window's effective copy-mode or copy-mode-vi table, including live custom bindings and mode-keys changes, then applies supported movement, history, word, prompt, selection, rectangle, search, and copy actions to the PTY-free retained output terminal. Stock emacs Escape and q cancel. Stock vi q cancels while Escape clears an active selection and leaves the view open. A live custom q or Escape binding wins. TerminalUiCommand::BeginSearch now opens the TUI's local search editor. Text entry, bounded and control-filtered paste, backspace, submit, cancel, next, and previous actions drive the command-output viewport, and the renderer uses display-cell width for the prompt and retained output. Search submission or cancellation closes only that local editor; the TUI consumes the matching repeat and release so the same physical key cannot also run an action in the retained output table. Protocol v79 adds output_id to the existing EventPayload::CommandOutput tag 11. The daemon assigns each real output actor a nonzero daemon-lifetime-global monotonic ID and keeps it on live frames, resync frames, and closes. ClientCore retains an ID watermark, ignores stale frames and closes, and prevents a closed actor from reappearing after mailbox coalescing or a resync race. The watermark resets when the client adopts a new handshake because a restarted daemon begins a new ID lifetime. Its confirm route runs before detach, sidebar, prompt, chooser, browser, terminal, paste, and mouse handling. Enter follows default_yes with any decoded modifier; named navigation and function input plus key releases are consumed without closing. After a reply, a pending gate retains input ownership until the daemon publishes None, and the reply key's repeat and release cannot escape after close. Accepted commands mutate unique options, rejected options stay empty for a one-second settle, prompt open and close are visible, and one blocking byte sentinel proves no tested reply key reached the pane before a final z releases it. Unit coverage proves state seeding and reconnect reset, single main or sidebar rendering, hidden-status placement, modifier and key rules, pending reply ownership, paste, pointer, and key lifecycle. No protocol or daemon change was needed. GPUI confirmation shares the pinned Meta and Enter rules while retaining its native platform, function, and named nontext handling. | `resource:crates/zz-client/src/core.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/command/confirm.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/state.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/app.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/input.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-tui/src/render.rs`, `file:compat/attached-client.sh`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:knowledge/designs/tmux-superset-roadmap.md` |
| `clients.tui-display-menu-overlay` | 2026-08-28 | Raw zz-tui now retains MenuState from ClientCore at construction, update, close, attachment replacement, and reconnect. The renderer layers the menu after workspace, chooser, or command-output bases, centres descriptor coordinates in the published client grid, clamps them to the outer terminal, draws the title, borders, item names, shortcuts, selected row, and disabled rows from one descriptor, suspends Kitty images, and hides the base cursor. | `clients.tui-display-popup-overlay` | 2026-08-28 | Raw zz-tui now seeds, updates, reconnects, replaces, closes, and resets PopupState from ClientCore while retaining the current synthetic terminal viewport only for the descriptor that owns it. One shared floating-layout resolver centres the published client grid, clamps it to the attached terminal, gives borderless popups their full frame, and insets every bordered family by one cell. | `commands.native-prefix-isolation` | 2026-08-24 | Exact canonical names and aliases resolve first. Non-exact lookups use tmux canonical names whenever any match exists and consult the guarded 19-name native roster only when tmux has no match. The daemon expands one immutable user-alias layer for a direct invocation before read-only authorization and reuses it for dispatch and hooks. Stored bind-key and set-hook lists execute their constructed commands without another user-alias lookup; read-only clients authorize that frozen chain before any effect. Typed `if-shell`, `run-shell`, and `confirm-before` callbacks also stay frozen after lexical construction. Typed `if-shell` and `run-shell` callbacks retain physical groups, stopping a failed group while continuing later physical lines; string callbacks remain one group. Typed `command-prompt` templates retain their structured constructed command list through submission without another user-alias lookup. String templates substitute raw source before a fresh parse and complete construction pass against the then-current alias table. Both prompt paths replace the first `%%` and every `%1`, with trailing-percent quoting; typed substitution preserves leaf-argument boundaries and physical groups, while string templates and free input form one group. `set-hook` and command-valued native set-option retain their intentional second construction stage. A typed `display-menu` action drops its structural wrapper before the fresh selection stage, while a quoted brace string remains literal. Non-exact attach prefixes execute through the interactive command path, static agent-send prefixes trigger stdin capture, and protocol v74 prepares Control command units against the live alias table before framing. The manifest gate derives the native roster from catalog minus the pinned oracle and checks every pinned canonical prefix; the strict 29-step scenario covers all 25 affected unique prefixes, exact catalog alias precedence, user command-alias expansion, and ambiguous list-commands exit parity. | `commands.tmux-name-extensions` | 2026-08-24 | The pin's outer send-keys grammar is c:FHKlMN:Rt:X. zz removed C, P, and o from the outer catalog and returns the exact unknown-flag error when they appear there; the tracked c, K, and R gaps remain under terminal.key-control, and M remains under mouse.bound-context. The copy-mode parser recognizes -C and -P on the pin's 14 copy-family grammar entries and -o on next-prompt and previous-prompt; -- terminates its flag scan. Invalid local flags, actions, and arity produce no command error or copy action and reset the copy-mode repeat prefix to 1. Existing CopyModeCopy clipboard, paste-buffer, and pipe fields retain their behavior. Execution for copy-line, copy-line-and-cancel, copy-pipe-line, and copy-pipe-line-and-cancel remains under terminal.key-control through semantic:send-keys-copy-command-shape. | `config.non-utf8-file-bytes` | 2026-09-01 | The startup and runtime loaders read config files as bytes and keep the pin's two input shapes apart. A runtime source-file matches cmd-source-file.c, which reads the file into a buffer and calls load_cfg_from_buffer, so parse_config_buffer_bytes applies the signed-char getc reading where 0xff is one EOF: it ends the current line, the file keeps parsing, and a second 0xff stops it. The startup -f file matches cfg.c load_cfg, which parses the open stdio file, so parse_config_file_bytes keeps 0xff inside the token and the command name stays unknown. A non-empty file that parses to no command now consumes the pin's cmdq_empty_command item, which cmd_source_file_done skips for a zero-length file, so control clients see the pin's hidden numbering for byte, comment-only, and blank files alike. For a file holding only 0xff, direct Command sourcing exits 0 with empty streams, direct Control emits one flags-1 success guard then consumes the hidden empty-command item and the ordinary source completion before the next guard, and a synchronous if-shell emits the parent and source guards with no visible diagnostic and continues the root file; none of these report `stream did not contain valid UTF-8` any more. Nothing substitutes lossy text: a parse whose surviving command name or argument still holds bytes that are not valid UTF-8 keeps the typed read error, because zz's typed UTF-8 command protocol cannot carry those bytes at all - the same limit `zz display-message -p` shows for a non-UTF-8 argv, which stays recorded as semantic:non-utf8-command-arguments under the accepted protocol.binary-streams group. | `config.parser-edge-cases` | 2026-08-29 | Config tilde expansion now follows the pin's parser-state transitions at a word start, inside an opening double quote, and immediately after an empty or nonempty closing quote. | `config.replayed-command-errors` | 2026-08-25 | Config replay now keeps source diagnostics and command runtime failures in one ordered report stream. Typed target failures and semantic errors from a syntactically valid set-option or set-window-option command use the pin's bare message instead of a file-prefixed parser diagnostic. Command clients receive stderr and exit 1. Protocol v76 Control clients receive each parser-owned failed replay command inside its own flags-1 error guard and set retval 1 for a later blank line, EOF, or nonself detach. The later control-mode.source-file-exit-status closure proves that an actual self-detach exits 0 and that a pending Return captured during a preceding non-detach command keeps its arrival-time snapshot ahead of later queued stdin. A Return observed while self-detach itself waits is discarded when the caller's Detached event arrives. Attached clients receive the capitalized warning form. A containing source-file propagates the same message and exit status while inner and outer later lines still run. Unknown command names and lexer or malformed command diagnostics retain the existing file-prefixed `%config-error` path; generic Warning identity remains open under control-mode.diagnostic-typing. Clientless startup still retains no invoking error channel and remains under config.startup-diagnostic-delivery. The later config.replayed-command-output closure preserves stdout before and after a runtime failure and batches only command-name and parser diagnostics into the transcript at their invocation boundary. Runtime, no-match, glob, and actual OS or path read failures retain their existing error channels. The later control-mode.indirect-source-frames closure adds inherited flags-1 framing for foreground shell-evaluated `if-shell`, immediate `if-shell -F` including `-bF`, and foreground `run-shell -C`. At this checkpoint immediate-hook and background-callback flags-0 framing remained open under control-mode.hook-command-frames and control-mode.background-inserted-command-frames. Later closures closed both groups. Non-UTF-8 config content remains under config.non-utf8-file-bytes. | `config.replayed-command-output` | 2026-08-25 | Runtime source-file now retains one stdout transcript per invocation. It parses every declared and globbed match before replay, appends the complete top-level -v batch in declared-path, glob, and physical-line order, replays the parsed files in the same order, then appends buffered command-name and parser diagnostics. Source no-match, glob, and actual OS or path read failures retain their existing error channels instead of joining the stdout transcript. Non-UTF-8 config content remains under config.non-utf8-file-bytes. Each nested source-file creates the same verbose, replay, command-diagnostic frame at its parent command's replay position, so nested frames run depth-first. This matches the pin's per-invocation batching and does not claim physical verbose and replay interleaving. Command clients receive sourced display-message -p, list-sessions, hook, and continuation output once on stdout. Successful output leaves stderr empty and status zero; a runtime failure keeps its original stderr and status 1 while stdout before, hook output, later output, and list output remain ordered. For syntactically valid successful replay and -v output, Interactive clients open one command-output view without duplicate Info or Warning events. Parser diagnostics may still publish their existing Warning summary. The attached fixture first proved transcript presentation and q dismissal; the later clients.tui-command-output-navigation closure added page and line movement, search, selection and copy, custom tables, and mode-key behavior. Direct Control replay keeps -v suppressed and uses its existing per-command guards. The later control-mode.indirect-source-frames closure extends flags-1 framing to synchronous foreground shell-evaluated if-shell, immediate if-shell -F including -bF, and foreground run-shell -C. At this checkpoint immediate-hook and background-callback flags-0 framing remained open under control-mode.hook-command-frames and control-mode.background-inserted-command-frames. Later closures closed both groups. One source-file invocation parses all top-level matches before replay: a bare assignment applies during parsing, affects a later file's conditional, and persists, while a replayed set-environment command runs too late to affect a later file's already parsed branch but persists after replay. With -n, neither assignment nor command effects apply, later parse-only files see the assignment as absent, and -v still reports the selected branch. Clientless startup creates no replay transcript, so the detached launcher keeps empty stdout and stderr. A synchronous invalid or runtime command error, a nested source depth refusal, or a loud source-file no-match or glob error with zero matched files drops only later siblings from that group; the next physical line still runs. The later config.replayed-command-output closure places each nested invocation's complete verbose, replay, and command-name or parser diagnostic transcript frame at this asynchronous source position. Source no-match, glob, and actual OS or path read failures keep their existing error channels at that position. The later source-file.nested-control-queue closure proved cross-depth containing-before-child order for parser-owned recursion. The later control-mode.indirect-source-frames closure covers synchronous foreground inserted lists. | `config.startup-diagnostic-delivery` | 2026-08-26 | Protocol v80 appends `EventPayload::StartupConfigCauses` at tail tag 49, bounded to 1,024 causes, 64 KiB each, and 1 MiB total with dedicated payload validation. Startup parses every root before replay. A missing implicit default is silent; explicit-root read failures, non-`NotFound` default-root failures, normalized nested read failures, parser and unknown-command diagnostics, unsupported and runtime failures, and successful `display-message -p` output are retained in root order with nested depth-first replay. List-style output stays discarded. Successful physical multiline commands use their completion line. A detached Command launch remains rc 0 with empty stdout and stderr and cannot drain the causes. Only the post-spawn Control connection advertises `startup-config-owner-v1`; it receives causes after `ServerHello` and before its first `%begin`. A Control connection to an existing daemon waits for attachment, then receives causes after `Attached` inside that attach frame. An attached Interactive client instead receives an ordered, UTF-8-safe 64 KiB preview in a PTY-free `configuration errors` view, with every Unicode control except LF and TAB replaced. A truncated preview ends with `... startup diagnostics truncated; restart in Control mode for full output`; full 1 MiB Interactive recovery is not promised. Startup output alone uses the pinned Ghostty 64 MiB byte history cap, while ordinary command output keeps its 100,000-byte setting. Eligible delivery is linearized globally. The post-spawn owner commits after the raw event is admitted. For an attached winner, the daemon commits the one-shot only after the `Attached` frame, diagnostic, resync, and mux options remain admitted; count or byte admission failure retains the causes and retires only the startup actor by exact ID. A daemon restart builds a fresh set. Control receives the raw bounded vector when it wins and prefixes each multiline cause once with `%config-error`, preserving raw continuation lines without changing retval. | `control-mode.async-command-output` | 2026-08-26 | Protocol v81 appends EventPayload::ControlCommandOutput at tail tag 50. A targetless or invalid-target foreground run-shell from Control publishes its captured stdout and nonzero or signal diagnostic to that exact originating client after the direct flags-1 response closes; a peer Control client receives nothing. The Control writer emits the payload raw, preserves embedded LF and percent-prefixed text literally, appends one LF when the payload lacks it, and does not change retained retval, so a nonzero run-shell still exits 0 for a fresh Control client. Direct and sourced commands emit an empty successful guard before the raw payload, then continue later commands or same-line sourced siblings in their own guards. Pinned tmux can crash its server when a foreground Control client disconnects while run-shell waits. zz treats that crash as out of scope, retains its server, and does not emulate the failure. Protocol framing, daemon routing, writer, serialized CLI, and sourced-order regressions pass.
