| `keys.client-focus-events` | 2026-08-25 | With focus-events enabled, writable ClientFocus runs through the modal prequeue, then session activity and FocusIn-only latest-geometry accounting, then synthetic Any dispatch. The daemon selects transient tables in chooser, copy-mode or command-output, and effective-root order. A transient Any binding wins; an unbound transient table falls back to the effective root without retiring the mode. A root Any binding runs when no transient mode applies. The daemon resolves attachment and pane context again after prompt submission before it dispatches the selected command. Disabled focus bypasses accounting and dispatch. Read-only focus retains its modal bypass, resolves the whole Any binding, authorizes every command before any effect, and rejects a mixed safe and unsafe chain atomically. Exact FocusIn and FocusOut remain invalid key names, and even injected exact bindings do not replace Any. Synthetic ingress preserves pending copy jump capture, numeric prefix state, repeat metadata and deadlines, table fallback and retirement, prefix synchronization, client isolation, and the next real key. The focused daemon cluster passes 9 of 9 tests, and an independent Codex read-only review returned CODE GO. ClientFocus is not CLI-drivable, so this closure makes no differential or canonical-suite claim. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/status-line.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `keys.copy-mode-action-and-repeat-fidelity` | 2026-08-25 | The emacs M-f default stores the pin's send-keys -X next-word-end action. Vi numeric capture consumes the buffered count at the first send or send-keys command whose option prefix contains -X. If that command already contains -N, its stored repeat wins; otherwise zz inserts one separate -N count pair immediately before the option argument containing -X. The engine does not scan onward after a stored -N, a command list with no qualifying -X leaves the count armed, and later actions do not inherit it. One exhaustive typed policy carries a u32 count through one flat TerminalViewAction::CopyModeCounted action: movements, jumps, matching brackets, and repeat-search execute count times; other-end swaps only for odd counts; select-line spans count logical lines; copy-end-of-line spans through the end of row N and copies once; other toggles, selection, copy, cancel, and later actions execute once. Counted raw key sends carry one repeat field instead of preallocating N tokens. Terminal delivery stops on the first full input queue. Browser events and both clients cap their native repeat path at MAX_BROWSER_KEY_REPEAT (9,999), because tmux has no browser pane. Direct -N parsing expands the last value, accepts 1 through UINT_MAX, preserves attached and clustered forms plus command abbreviations, and reports the pin's invalid, too-small, and too-large errors. Protocol v75 appends flat counted-copy tag 28 and browser-repeat tag 7; tests reject the removed recursive action tag and nested payloads. The strict send-keys-repeat differential covers separate, attached, clustered, duplicate, formatted, alias, prefix, and error forms. Invalid nonempty -X grammar returns the pin's neutral prefix value 1, represented as no buffered count, so the next digit starts a fresh prefix. Multi-digit vi capture remains bounded at 9,999 per client; bare no-key counts and `-N <n> -X` with no action remain pane-prefix residuals under terminal.key-control. The nine digit bindings retain their native copy-mode-repeat list-keys shape under keys.copy-mode-native-numeric-prefix; unrelated cursor-word, search, goto-line, and jump command shapes remain open under keys.copy-mode-binding-fidelity. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/interaction.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/session.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/message.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/keys.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/browser/view.rs`, `resource:crates/zz/src/browser/tui.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/copy-mode.md`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/send-keys-repeat.txt` |
| `keys.copy-mode-defaults` | 2026-08-25 | Added the six remaining stock emacs keys whose typed actions already existed: C-[ runs cancel, C-k runs copy-pipe-end-of-line-and-cancel, C-w runs copy-pipe-and-cancel, N runs search-reverse, R runs rectangle-toggle, and n runs search-again. Each binding stores the pin's single send-keys -X command and carries no repeat bit. KeyEngine tests cover the six bindings and retain the prior Escape, M-w, and C-g bindings; exact equality with the audited stock key set proves that none overwrote another key. Mux tests cover cancel, both copy variants, forward and reverse search repetition, and rectangle toggle as typed terminal effects. The 17 absent stock keys remain under keys.copy-mode-prompt-defaults and keys.copy-mode-unsupported-default-actions. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/compat_manifest_tests.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/copy-mode.md` |
| `keys.copy-mode-navigation-defaults` | 2026-08-25 | Added the pin's 13 previously absent emacs navigation keys with exact send-keys -X command shapes and nonrepeat metadata: C-Down, C-M-Down, C-M-Up, C-M-f, C-Up, End, Home, M-<, M->, M-Down, M-R, M-Up, and Space. Their existing typed actions cover line scrolling, semantic-prompt navigation, matching brackets, line endpoints, history endpoints, half pages, top-line positioning, and page-down without a new terminal action or UI flow. KeyEngine and mux effect tests cover every key and action, and shifted Alt input reaches M-R, M-<, and M->. At this historical closure 23 stock keys remained: six supported non-navigation defaults, ten prompt-backed defaults, and seven keys behind unsupported actions. The later keys.copy-mode-defaults closure reduced the current residual to 17. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/compat_manifest_tests.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/copy-mode.md` |
| `keys.copy-mode-prompt-defaults` | 2026-09-01 | All ten keys are installed, so the emacs copy table no longer swallows M-1 through M-9 and g. M-1 through M-9 arm zz's per-client numeric capture with the same `copy-mode-repeat <digit>` command the vi digits use, and the capture rule that only vi digits could extend now covers both copy tables, so M-1 2 accumulates to 12 exactly as the pin's PROMPT_NUMERIC prompt accumulates digits after its -I seed. The next copy action consumes the count through the same engine path as vi: the count lands as one `-N <count>` pair before the argument containing -X, which is what the pin's `{ send-keys -N "%%" }` block does when it writes wme->prefix for the following action. g opens the same goto-line prompt vi ':' opens, and both now carry a structured `{ send-keys -X goto-line -- "%%" }` command block instead of the old string template, so submission edits the leaf argument in place and a typed line number cannot inject a second command. The exact stored command shape in this group's acceptance was not reached and did not vanish: the pin renders both families through `command-prompt -P`, whose pane-cell prompt is loudly unsupported by the accepted `prompt.pane-rendered` decision. The nine M-digit shape divergences moved to `keys.copy-mode-native-numeric-prefix`, which already owns that stance for the vi digits, and `binding:copy-mode:g` moved to `keys.copy-mode-binding-fidelity` beside `binding:copy-mode-vi::`. Nothing wire-reachable changed. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/compat_manifest_tests.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |
| `keys.copy-mode-unsupported-default-actions` | 2026-09-02 | All seven stock keys are installed with the pin's stored `send-keys -X <action>` shape and its nonrepeat metadata. The last two, `copy-mode-vi r` and `copy-mode r`, waited on refresh-toggle, which is a re-sync of the frozen backing rather than a cursor move. window_copy_refresh_start refuses a view of another pane and view-mode and does nothing when the refresh is already running; window_copy_refresh_timer skips a tick unless the pane has unseen output with no selection and no cursor drag; and window_copy_do_refresh re-clones the backing, keeps the view on the row it was already showing, and only follows new output when oy is 0 and the cursor is on the last row. zz keeps the frozen revision on the client's terminal view and the terminal worker owns no timers, so the daemon runs one 50ms thread while any copy session asks for a refresh and the engine decides which ticks do anything; the three toggle actions carry the bit on both the frozen view and the daemon's copy session, and CopyModeAction gained RefreshOn, RefreshOff, RefreshToggle and the daemon-driven RefreshRevision tick, which is the v94 protocol bump. Measured on the pin against a pane printing a line every 50ms with an attached pty client: `#{scroll_position}` and `#{copy_cursor_line}` both hold after entry with the refresh off, the position climbs after `refresh-on` while the cursor line does not move, `refresh-toggle` freezes it and a second toggle restarts it, a live selection holds it even with the refresh running and clearing the selection resumes it, `refresh-off` freezes it, and with the view at the bottom and the cursor on the last row the position stays 0 instead of climbing. compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-refresh.txt asserts those thirteen facts as comparisons rather than absolute rows, because the two clients render panes of different heights, and both binaries answer identically, including `list-keys` rendering `send-keys -X refresh-toggle` for `r` in both tables. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/compat_manifest_tests.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/key.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/interaction.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/key-tables.md`, `file:compat/scenarios/copy-mode-stock-action-keys.txt`, `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/session.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/copy-mode-refresh.txt`, `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/copy-mode-refresh.sh` |

| `mux.chain-parse-abort` | 2026-08-29 | Each config file is now parsed, alias-expanded, and statically validated as one construction unit before any command from that file runs. The first construction failure retains earlier bare environment assignments but discards every command effect from that file. Parse-only source-file -n performs the same validation using the pre-file environment for expansion, while applying neither assignments nor commands. Startup roots and files matched by one source invocation remain independent file units constructed in path order before batch replay, so one bad file drops only itself and later siblings continue. A nested child receives a fresh construction snapshot; its failure drops the child without stopping its parent's later physical groups. Runtime target and effect errors remain sequential and prune only their own physical group. Control reports one located config error without a sourced-command guard. Verbose source output includes successful alias-subparse traces and completed physical groups before the first failure, including parse-only files. | `options.client-attach-and-focus` | 2026-09-02 | Closed on 2026-09-02 against pinned tmux d77c9dc6 with both binaries driven on real ptys (scenarios default-client-command and focus-follows-mouse). `default-client-command`: server_client_dispatch_command sees argc == 0 for a client that reaches the server with no command of its own and queues server_client_default_command, which reads the option as a command list and inserts it after the callback, so the bare client runs the list and exits with its status. zz's launcher on PATH hard-coded `new-session -A` for an empty command line; it now marks every command line it forwards with the private `--bootstrap-launcher-client` argument, and the bundled executable, on an empty command line carrying that mark, asks the daemon for `show-options -sqv default-client-command`, parses the answer with the same config parser a bound command string goes through, and runs the result through the ordinary command-chain path, so the list picks up TUI attach routing, the daemon spawn, and the error channels an explicit argument gets. Measured on both binaries: `display-message -p custom` writes `custom` and exits 0; the two-command list `display-message -p one ; display-message -p two` writes both lines in order; `new-session -d -s NAME` creates that session and lets the client exit 0; and an unknown command is refused when the option is set, with the pin's `unknown command: no-such-command` on stderr, status 1, and the stored list left alone, which zz already matched. The acceptance clause's read-only half was re-scoped after measurement. It read "keeping the pin's read-only refusal for a list that is not all read-only commands"; that branch of server_client_default_command cannot be reached by any client the pin's own command line can produce. CLIENT_READONLY is reachable on the pin before any command is dispatched: server_acl_join sets `c->flags \|= CLIENT_READONLY` for a user whose ACL entry carries SERVER_ACL_READONLY (server-access.c), and server.c calls it when the client connects, so a second Unix user granted `server-access -a -r USER` who runs a bare `tmux -S sock` reaches server_client_default_command with the flag on and gets `client is read-only` for a list that is not all read-only; attach-session -r, switch-client -r and a later refresh-client -f read-only set it directly too. zz cannot produce a read-only client at join because its socket is single-user 0600 and `server-access` is accepted-native under command:server-access, so no zz client the launcher path can produce carries the flag, which is why nothing in this group claims the refusal - not because the pin branch is unreachable. The pin's usage string is `tmux [-2CDhlNuVv] [-c shell-command] [-f file] [-L socket-name] [-S socket-path] [-T features] [command [flags]]` and `tmux -r` is answered with `unknown option -- r` and status 1, measured on the pin. One recorded divergence is deliberately kept: the option's own default, `new-session` with no arguments, still becomes zz's `new-session -A`, which is the create-or-attach launcher behavior knowledge/tmux/divergences.md already records as silent and product-friendly. Any other stored value runs verbatim, so setting the option is what a script sees, and setting it to `new-session` restores the pin's create-another-session launcher only by spelling it differently. `focus-follows-mouse`: server_client_check_mouse turns a KEYC_TYPE_MOUSEMOVE resolved inside a pane that is not the window's active one into window_redraw_active_switch plus window_set_active_pane while the option is on, and tty.c forces MODE_MOUSE_ALL on the client so a real terminal reports motion at all. The value now rides the option map every client already receives: protocol v94 appends MuxOptionKey::FocusFollowsMouse, the daemon stamps the session-effective value into effective_mux_options beside `mouse`, a set at any session scope raises MuxOptionChanged from stored_scalar_execution, and both clients gate their pointer route on it - the raw TUI in handle_mouse before it decides who owns the event, the GPUI workspace with an on_mouse_move listener on every inactive pane surface. Two measurements shaped that placement. The pin reads the option before the mouse key becomes a binding, so the switch runs with `mouse` off as well: on an attached 79x24 pin client with `mouse off` and `focus-follows-mouse on`, a bare SGR motion report over the right pane still selected it, which is why zz's check sits ahead of its own mouse-option route rather than inside the workspace branch. And a report with a button held is a KEYC_TYPE_MOUSEDRAG, which never reaches that arm, so a drag leaves the active pane alone on both binaries. The scenario drives a 79-column client on both engines, where the one-column divider lands on column 39 and the right pane on columns 40..78, and reads the result through #{pane_active} from outside the client. One hook divergence is recorded rather than copied: the pin's server_client_check_mouse calls window_set_active_pane directly, so a focus-follows-mouse switch fires window-pane-changed but NOT after-select-pane, while zz spends the switch as a `select-pane -t %N` command (the raw TUI's pointer_focus_follows_mouse and the GPUI on_pane_pointer_focus both go through pane_select_command), so after-select-pane also fires on zz - once per motion report that crosses into a new pane. | `pane.selection-state` | 2026-09-01 | select-pane -m, -M, -d, -e, -g, and -P are promoted out of the unsupported catalog and the four marked formats now read live state. Measured on the pin with a throwaway server: the mark is one server-global tuple (server.c server_set_marked and cmd_find_valid_state), so `-m` on a second pane moves it, `-m` on the pane that already holds it clears it through server_is_marked, `-M` clears whatever holds it, and killing the marked pane drops it in window_lost_pane. `pane_marked` is 1 only on the holder, `pane_marked_set` is 1 server-wide while any mark exists, `session_marked` and `window_marked_flag` are 1 only for the owning session and window, and window_printable_flags emits M between the `-` last-window flag and `Z`, so a marked zoomed current window reads `*MZ`. `-m` refuses a pane hidden by zoom (window_pane_is_visible) while `-M` still clears through the same target. zz models one window per session, so the pin's four-part find state collapses to the session and window the mark was taken in plus the pane, and validity is checked on read the way server_check_marked defers to cmd_find_valid_state, which walks marked_pane.s->windows for marked_pane.wl and ends at window_has_pane: break-pane and join-pane move the marked pane into another window and drop the mark, a swap inside the same window keeps it and follows the new index, move-window and swap-window keep it inside one session because server_link_window and cmd_swap_window_exec retarget marked_pane.wl, and both drop it across sessions because neither retargets marked_pane.s. Measured on the pin, `move-window -d -s w:0 -t other:5` and `swap-window -d -s w:1 -t other:1` leave the moved window's pane reading `0:0:0:0` for pane_marked, pane_marked_set, session_marked, and window_marked_flag, which a zz-mux test and the `pane-selection-marked` differential both carry. cmd_select_pane_exec order is reproduced: `-l` first (routing -e and -d at the last pane), then -m/-M, then -P, then -g, then the directional search, then -e before -d, then -T, then the selection. `-P` writes the pane-local window-style and window-active-style with options_set_string, which never validates, so `select-pane -P bogus=zzz` stores the raw string and exits 0 where `set-option -p window-style bogus=zzz` answers `invalid style: bogus=zzz` and exits 1; the deprecated `-g` prints the pane's effective window-style and returns before selection, while a bare `-P` falls through and selects. Input-off enforcement already lived in the daemon input path (resolve_input_sinks), so -d and -e only needed the mux state they toggle. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/model.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/formats.rs`, `file:compat/scenarios/pane-selection-marked.txt`, `file:compat/scenarios/pane-selection-input-style.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |

| `targets.exact-match-prefix` | 2026-09-01 | The resolver now records which slot of cmd_find_target a token reached. A session or window slot strips the leading equals sign into an exact-match flag and maps the window alias table; a colon-less token for a pane target lands in the pane slot, so the window and session lookups that run after the pane miss search for it exactly as written, alias table included. Every measured row now matches the pin byte for byte on a throwaway server and a scratch daemon: `set-option -t =w history-limit 1234` answers `no such session: =w` and exits 1, `-t =alp` answers `no such session: =alp` instead of degrading into a session prefix search, `set-option -w -t =win0` answers `no such window: =win0`, `set-option -p -t =w` answers `no such pane: =w`, `kill-pane -t =w` and `select-pane -t =win0` answer `can't find pane:` with the equals sign kept, while `set-option -t =w:` succeeds because the colon moves the token into the session slot, and `set-window-option -t =win0` and `has-session -t =w` succeed because their own target types put it in the window and session slots. The cmd-find diagnostics keep reporting the stripped name (`has-session -t =wi` answers `can't find session: wi`, `kill-window -t =wi` answers `can't find window: wi`) while the option-scope diagnostics echo the raw `-t` argument, because the pin resolves an option target with CMD_FIND_CANFAIL and then lets options_scope_from_flags name the slot the option's own scope needs. That rewrite also fixes three adjacent messages measured the same way: `set-option -t nope`, `-t 9.9`, `-w -t :nope`, and `-p -t :nope` now answer with the pin's exact text. One residue case stays divergent and is not claimed here: the pin's `set-option -t :nope history-limit` succeeds on the retained session while zz still fails, because zz's option target derives its session from a resolved window instead of the componentwise CANFAIL state. | `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/model.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `file:compat/scenarios/pane-exact-target-prefix.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/commands.md` |
| `terminal.key-client-selection` | 2026-09-01 | send-keys -c is a real catalog option and resolves the pin's target client. MuxEffect::TerminalView and MuxEffect::CopyModeRepeat now carry an optional target_client, and the daemon resolves it through the same find_attached_client_with_aliases path the popup and menu commands use, falling back to the invoking client when the flag is absent. The read-only guard moved with it: prepare_command_request and the bound-key preflight in execute_key_commands both ask read_only_guard_client which client cmd-send-keys.c would test, which is the -c client rather than the invoker, and a target that matches nothing leaves no client to test, matching CMD_CLIENT_CFLAG\|CMD_CLIENT_CANFAIL. The bound-key half was caught at the cycle-6 gate: key_bindings_dispatch admits a send-keys binding on a read-only client because cmd_send_keys_entry carries CMD_READONLY and cmd_send_keys_exec then tests tc, so on the pin a read-only client pressing `bind-key -n z send-keys -c <writer tty> -t %0 -l K` and `bind-key -n y send-keys -c /dev/pts/nonexistent -t %0 -l N` delivers KN while zz refused both against the invoker's flag; the scenario now drives those two bindings plus an un-flagged `send-keys -t %0 -l U` from the read-only pty client and both binaries land exactly KN. Proved differentially by compat/scenarios/smoke/send-keys-client-selection.txt, which attaches two real pty clients, one read-write and one attached with -r, and runs 21 checks that both binaries answer identically: a resolved read-write client delivers at status 0; the read-only client answers `client is read-only` at status 1 and delivers nothing even though the invoker is an unattached command client; `-c /dev/pts/nonexistent-client` and `-c ''` are quiet at status 0 and still deliver, so the pane holds exactly ACD; the /dev-stripped tty spelling selects the same read-only client; and `-c <read-only> -X cursor-up` is accepted at status 0 because the guard is `tc != NULL && CLIENT_READONLY && !args_has(args, 'X')`, while the same client without -X is refused again. Unsafe -X actions selected onto a read-only client, `-X begin-selection` and `-X copy-pipe`, are refused at status 1 per clients.read-only-local-view-actions, where the pin answers 0 and posts `client is read-only` as a status message on that client because window_copy_command only checks WINDOW_COPY_CMD_FLAG_READONLY inside the mode. One divergence stays open and is recorded rather than closed: with no -c at all the pin runs cmd_find_current_client, which for an unattached command client picks the attached client with the newest activity_time, so a bare `send-keys` can be refused by a read-only peer's flag; zz keeps using the invoking client. That selection is activity-ordered and untestable without a timing race, so the scenario asserts only the -c forms. | `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md`, `resource:third_party/tmux-reference/UPSTREAM.md`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/smoke/send-keys-client-selection.txt`, `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/send-keys-client-selection.sh`, `file:compat/scenarios/smoke/fixtures/pty-drive.py` |
| `terminal.key-reset` | 2026-09-01 | `send-keys -R` is implemented and the catalog flag is promoted. zz composes the pin's `screen_write_reset` out of VT bytes instead of calling libghostty's `reset`, which is RIS and discards history: a bare ST abandons any half-parsed string sequence without printing a cell, an explicit SGR and charset reset restores the pen the way `input_reset_cell` does, DECSTBM drops the scroll region, and because the pinned libghostty (20c3eae0) ignores DECSTR outright (its `CSI p` arm implements only DECRQM and logs `ignoring unimplemented CSI p with intermediates` for `ESC[!p`), every flag the pin's `s->mode = MODE_CURSOR\|MODE_WRAP` assignment drops gets its own sequence: DECAWM and DECTCEM come back on, and DECCKM, IRM, DECOM, LNM, DECKPAM, cursor blink, synchronized output, theme updates, the kitty keyboard flags, modifyOtherKeys, and the mouse, focus, and bracketed-paste modes go off. TBC 3 plus one HTS every eight columns reproduces `screen_reset_tabs`, a scroll-up sized to the used visible rows reproduces `grid_view_clear_history`, DECSC after the final home mirrors `input_reset_cell`'s `old_cx = old_cy = 0`, and OSC 104, 110, 111, and 112 reproduce `colour_palette_clear`. Measured against the pin on 2026-09-01 with a 20x6 pane printing twenty lines: history_size goes 15 to 20, the cursor lands at 0,0, `capture-pane -S -` still holds L01 through L20 followed by six blank rows, a custom tab stop at column 4 goes back to the default 8, and a pane whose scroll region was narrowed scrolls the full screen again afterwards. Four zz-terminal tests assert each of those; a fifth, `pane_reset_drops_the_modes_the_pin_clears_with_send_keys_r`, asserts the pin's mode numbers (`keypad_cursor_flag` 1 to 0 so Up encodes `ESC[A`, `insert_flag` 1 to 0 so `ab\rX` captures `Xb`, the extended-key state dropped so Escape encodes a bare ESC, and the saved cursor back at 0,0), which the differential cannot see because zz's `keypad_cursor_flag`, `insert_flag`, and `origin_flag` formats are constant zero; and `send_keys_reset_runs_before_the_requested_key_delivery` asserts the command shape the pin's exec has: `-R` alone resets and sends nothing, `-R` with strings resets and then injects exactly what a bare `send-keys` would, and `-X` returns before the `-R` block ever runs. The differential scenario `send-keys-reset` runs the whole thing against the pin and is clean on all six axes at strict geometry. One qualification the acceptance did not name: the pin's clear runs through `screen_write_clearscreen`, so it only pushes into history because `scroll-on-clear` defaults on; zz reproduces that default unconditionally because the option itself is parked in `options.pane-engine-knobs`, which is where turning it off belongs. One mode survives by design: the pin keeps `MODE_KEYS_EXTENDED` when `extended-keys` is `always`, and zz drops it unconditionally because that option lives in `options.client-terminal-negotiation`. The redraw the acceptance names is the Content snapshot the terminal worker publishes after the reset; the pin's `PANE_STYLECHANGED` and `PANE_THEMECHANGED` flags have no observable effect after its own mode reset clears `MODE_THEME_UPDATES`, so nothing models them. `-R` reaches terminal panes only: zz-only browser, picker, agent, and editor panes have no pin equivalent and are skipped. Nothing wire-reachable changed: the reset rides an internal MuxEffect and a terminal worker command, not a protocol message. | `resource:crates/zz-terminal/src/session.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-mux/src/command.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-protocol/src/catalog.rs`, `resource:crates/zz-daemon/src/daemon.rs`, `scenario:compat/scenarios/send-keys-reset.txt`, `resource:knowledge/tmux/divergences.md` |

| `tracker.args-parse-command-prompt` | 2026-08-28 | Protocol v84 lexical command-block positions now drive `command-prompt`'s pinned command-or-string rule without another wire change. The command accepts zero or one template positional as a string or typed block, while `-I`, `-p`, `-t`, and `-T` values remain strings. Every lexical typed block constructs recursively before its parent command's name, callback type, or arity validation. A typed template retains that frozen constructed list through submission; a string template retains raw source and constructs after answer substitution against the then-current alias table. Typed substitution edits structured leaf arguments without reparsing answer text, preventing quote or semicolon injection. String substitution runs against the raw template before parsing, then preflights the complete parsed result before any command executes. Both paths replace the first `%%` and every `%1`; a trailing `%` quotes `"`, `\`, `$`, `;`, and `~` in the inserted answer. Typed callbacks retain physical source groups, stopping the failed group while continuing later physical lines. String templates and free input form one group. String-template diagnostics retain the originating source path and line after substitution. Empty typed templates execute no commands. Interactive menu ordering remains unchanged.
